# U0824 | Environment in news - reports, indices, species, protected areas, climate negotiations
- **Rows:** G-13 - Environment in news - reports, indices, species, protected areas, climate negotiations; S4-11 - National - environment: reports, species, protected areas, climate initiatives
- **Exams:** G1-Prelims + G2-Screening | **Tier:** HIGH, 3 pp (~1,350 words) | **PYQs:** APPSC 24, other exams 10, last APPSC year 2025, latest papers 5
- **Keywords:**
  1. *New species discovered -> type + location + discovering institution (Impatiens karuppusamyi, burrowing frogs, orchids) [I]
  2. *Environmental alliances and initiatives -> lead country/ministry + members (Mangrove Alliance for Climate, Global River Cities Alliance, NDCTIA, International Nitrogen Initiative) [I]
  3. *Conservation designations -> designating body + site (IUCN Hope Spots, IUGS geological heritage sites, Ramsar sites, tiger reserves, biosphere reserves) [I]
  4. *Environmental disasters abroad -> country + emergency declared (oil spills, glacier loss, wildfires, floods) [I]
  5. *Species conservation measures - state schemes on human-wildlife conflict, radio-tagging, rhino declarations, IUCN status changes, Project Cheetah, Operation Olivia [I]
  6. *Air quality in news - cities ranked, AQI category, report source (World Air Quality Report, NCAP) [I]
  7. *Environment reports -> releasing agency + India's rank (State of India's Environment, CCPI, EPI, Living Planet) [I]
  8. +Climate negotiations - COP number, venue, outcomes, negotiating blocs (BASIC, loss and damage fund, NDCs, carbon-negative countries) [I]
  9. Cyclones and extreme weather in news - naming country, landfall region [I]
  10. *Climate and environment initiatives/alliances -> sponsor + members + objective (International Nitrogen Initiative, Mangrove Alliance for Climate, NDC Transport Initiative for Asia) [II]
  11. *New species discovered -> type (flora/fauna), place of discovery, discovering institute [II]
  12. *Energy and environment indices -> publisher + top states/India's rank (State Energy Efficiency Index, Climate Risk Index, Climate Change Performance Index) [II]
  13. *Wildlife conflict and conservation schemes -> state + species (elephant, rhino, pangolin, turtle protection operations) [II]
  14. *Environmental convention COPs -> venue + decisions (CMS, CITES, Ramsar) [II]
  15. *Geo-heritage and natural sites recognised -> site + state (IUGS geological heritage sites) [II]
  16. *Pollution and disasters in news - oil spills, air-quality hotspots, cyclones -> place [II]
  17. +National climate initiatives -> ministry + objective (MISHTI, Mission LiFE, Green Credit Programme, NDC and net-zero targets) [II]
  18. New protected areas - tiger reserves, Ramsar sites, biosphere reserves, national parks -> state [II]
- **NEW from 2024-25 papers:**
  N1. Natural and biological phenomena in news -> rare events, cicada broods, ice volcanoes, fire tornadoes, with place and recurrence interval
- **Latest-paper questions:** G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (New species); G2 Screening 2024 (NEW: Natural phenomena in news); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Convention/Conference); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (New species); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Places in news)

### Nameri Tiger Reserve gets CA|TS accreditation
## 1. Key facts
- Nameri TR (Sonitpur, Assam; NP since 1998; Jia Bhoroli river) — CA|TS Approved
- CA|TS: Conservation Assured | Tiger Standards — launched at 1st Asian Parks Congress, Japan, 2013; 7 pillars, 17 elements (first 5 CA, last 2 TS)
## 2. 15 CA|TS reserves in India
| State | Reserves |
| Assam | Kaziranga, Manas, Orang, Nameri |
| West Bengal | Sundarbans |
| Madhya Pradesh | Kanha, Panna, Satpura |
| Maharashtra | Pench |
## 3. Background
- Project Tiger 1 April 1973 (9 reserves); NTCA 2006
- AITE 2022: 3,682 tigers (min 3,167); MP 785, Karnataka 563, Uttarakhand 560; Tiger Day 29 July
- Tigers: 3,682 (2022 estimate) vs 1,411 (2006); some notes quote 3,167 = the camera-trapped minimum count
- Tiger reserves: cover 84,488 sq km (~2.6% of India); up from 9 reserves in 1973
## 4. Full list — 24 CA|TS-accredited reserves
- ⚠ The 10 Oct source listed 15; the 12 Oct source gives 24 CA|TS-accredited tiger reserves (use 24)
| State | Reserves |
| Assam | Manas, Kaziranga, Orang, Nameri |
| Madhya Pradesh | Satpura, Pench, Kanha, Panna, Bandhavgarh |
| Maharashtra | Pench, Melghat, Tadoba–Andhari, Navegaon–Nagzira |
| West Bengal | Sundarbans |
## 5. Nameri Wild Classroom
- Assam Forest Department launched "Nameri Wild Classroom" at Nameri TR — experiential conservation education for students from Class 8 onwards
- Tiger IUCN status Endangered; Kaziranga and Manas are UNESCO sites
### Short-Lived Climate Pollutants (SLCPs)
## 1. Key facts
- SLCPs cause up to 45% of present warming; IPCC — rapid cuts can avoid up to 0.6°C by 2050
- Also called: super pollutants, Short-Lived Climate Forcers
- Major warming SLCPs: black carbon, methane, tropospheric ozone, HFCs
- ⚠ Black carbon is not a greenhouse gas — a particle that absorbs radiation; sulphate aerosols cool
- ⚠ Tropospheric ozone is not directly emitted — formed from methane, NOx, VOCs in sunlight
- Methane: ~80 times CO2 warming over 20 years; second-largest contributor after CO2
- Stratospheric ozone ~10–50 km; tropospheric surface–10 km
- Ozone causes loss of ~110 million tonnes staple crops/year; air pollution ~7 million premature deaths/year globally
## 2. India
- Air pollution: 1.67 million deaths in 2019 (17.8%), loss US$36.8 billion (1.36% of GDP)
- Methane (2016, Third BUR): Agriculture 73.96%, Waste 14.46%, Energy 10.62%, Industry 0.96%
- ⚠ India has not joined the Global Methane Pledge — calls farm methane "survival emissions"
## 3. International framework
| Instrument | Fact |
| Montreal Protocol | 1987 — ozone-depleting substances |
| Kigali Amendment | 2016; India ratified Sept 2021; freeze HFCs by 2028, cut 85% by 2047; can avoid 0.5°C by 2100 |
| Global Methane Pledge | COP26 Glasgow 2021; cut 30% from 2020 levels by 2030; 159 countries + EC |
| Climate and Clean Air Coalition | UNEP-led; India joined 2019 |
## 4. India's measures
- Air Act 1981; NAAQS 2009 (12 pollutants); National AQI 2015; GRAP 2017 (Delhi-NCR)
- NCAP: 130 cities, 24 States/UTs; 2025–26: 65 cities within PM2.5 limit 40 µg/m³; Delhi 102 µg/m³; only 14 met PM10 (60 µg/m³)
- PMUY: >10.5 crore LPG connections; GOBARdhan; SRI, DSR, alternate wetting & drying
### National Coastal Mission 2.0 — implementation guidelines
## 1. Key facts
- Released by: Union MoEFCC Minister, New Delhi
- Period: 2025–26 to 2030–31; ₹767 crore; Central Sector Scheme

- Integrates NCM 1.0 (2021–22 to 2025–26); framework for Marine Spatial Planning (MSP), climate resilience, marine pollution, Blue Economy
### India's first high-altitude Snow Leopard Safari — Leh
## 1. Key facts
- Launched 7 October 2026, Leh, Ladakh (Wildlife Week); LG Vinai Kumar Saxena flagged off 6 open-back vehicles
- Implemented by: SHAN Society (Snow Leopard and High-Altitude Nature Conservation) + Ladakh Forest Dept
- SPAI 2024: India 718 snow leopards — Ladakh 477 (highest)
## 2. Background
- *Panthera uncia*; IUCN Vulnerable; WPA Schedule I; CITES Appendix I
- State animal of Himachal Pradesh; UT animal of Ladakh; International Snow Leopard Day 23 October; Project Snow Leopard 2009
- Ladakh UT since 31 October 2019

## 1. Key facts
- Union Environment Minister released two captive-bred male GIBs at Sam, Desert National Park, near Jaisalmer
- DNP: Thar Desert, >3,160 sq km; rainfall <100 mm; Jurassic fossils; extinct salt-lake beds
- GIB: *Ardeotis nigriceps*; IUCN Critically Endangered; Rajasthan State Bird; threat — power lines

### New plant species Didymocarpus nautiyalii — Arunachal Pradesh
## 1. Key facts
- Found in: Shi-Yomi district, Arunachal Pradesh
- Family: Gesneriaceae; herb ~8 cm; leathery leaves, blue-violet flowers; moist rocks above 1,000 m
- Named after: Prof. Sunil Nautiyal, former Director, G.B. Pant National Institute of Himalayan Environment (Almora, Uttarakhand; under MoEFCC)
### Uttarakhand's first real-time glacial lake early warning system — Vasundhara Lake
## 1. Key facts
- Where: Vasundhara Lake, Chamoli district, Uttarakhand — first real-time GLOF early warning system in the State
- Monitors water level, weather, snow depth, flow, seismic activity; satellite communication (no mobile network); solar-powered
## 2. Background
- Uttarakhand: capital Dehradun, summer capital Gairsain

## 1. Key facts
- Where: Deendayal Port Authority, Gandhidham–Kandla, Gujarat (Gulf of Kutch; formerly Kandla Port)
- Capacity: 150 tonnes/day; green marine fuel
- E-methanol: renewable electricity + water + biogenic CO2

## 1. Key facts

### Record melting of Aletsch Glacier 2026
## 1. Key facts
- Aletsch (Switzerland) — largest and longest glacier in the Alps; part of Swiss Alps Jungfrau–Aletsch UNESCO site; valley glacier
- Swiss glaciers lost 5.5% of remaining ice in 2026 — second-highest after 2022 (per GLAMOS and SCNAT)
- Causes: low winter snowfall + record summer heatwaves

## 1. Key facts

- Other volcanoes: Mayon, Taal, Pinatubo; capital Manila, Peso

- Nagar Van Yojana: target 1,000 by 2026–27 (626 supported); Aravalli Green Wall: 6.31 million ha; Ek Ped Maa Ke Naam: 262.4 crore saplings (Dec 2025)

### Nepal flash flood — rock–ice collapse
## 1. Event
- Date: August 26, 2026 — rock–ice collapse near Nepal–China border
- Toll: 579 deaths in Nepal; ~2,000 missing; 93,000+ need assistance; Kailash Mansarovar pilgrims missing
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say the toll was 1,259 (flash floods, Bhotekoshi-Trishuli rivers, Nepal, 26 Aug 2026)
- Initially read as a 4.4-magnitude quake; USGS said signal was from the collapse; energy equal to ~magnitude 5.2
- Material plunged ~1 km; ⚠ not a conventional GLOF — a rock–ice collapse-triggered debris flood
- Nepal: ~475 MW hydropower capacity lost
- 13 hydropower projects damaged (flash floods, Bhotekoshi-Trishuli rivers)
- China: Gyirong Port (Tibet) buried — upgraded at $175.8 million
- India: Rivers Gandak, Kosi, Ganga; Bihar and UP alert; Valmikinagar Barrage watched; Maharajganj, Kushinagar; India sent 47.5 tonnes of relief
## 2. India's Himalayan hazard measures
- National GLOF Risk Mitigation Programme: ₹150 crore — Arunachal Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand
- NDMA Guidelines on GLOFs: 2020; Dam Safety Act: 2021
- National Landslide Risk Mitigation Project: ₹1,000 crore, 15 States
### Pichavaram mangroves — economic valuation
## 1. Study
- Study: Economic Valuation and Blue Carbon Assessment of Ecosystem Services in the Pichavaram Mangrove Forest (Tamil Nadu)
- Total Economic Value: ₹2,485.38 crore (~₹1.83 crore/hectare)
- Survey of 302 households in 5 villages (MGR Thittu, Kalaignar Nagar, Chinnavaikal, Patriadi, Mudasalodai)
| Service | Value |
| Soil organic carbon | ₹1,224.63 crore (largest) |
| Carbon stock in vegetation | ₹387.77 crore |
| Oxygen regulation | ₹181.22 crore |
| Coastal protection | ₹147.17 crore |
- Vegetation carbon: 79.449 t/ha (~291.33 t CO₂/ha); soil organic carbon: 251.14 t/ha; combined carbon value ₹1,612.40 crore
- ⚠ Most mangrove carbon is below ground in sediments
## 2. Background
- ISFR 2023: mangrove cover 4,992 sq km (~0.15% of area; 4,628 in 2013); West Bengal > Gujarat > A&N Islands
- IUCN 2024: 50% of world's mangrove ecosystems at risk of collapse
- MISHTI (Mangrove Initiative for Shoreline Habitats and Tangible Incomes): launched 2023, ₹100 crore
- Adaptations: pneumatophores (breathing roots), prop roots, vivipary; mangroves called "Kidneys of the Planet"
- Bhitarkanika (Odisha) — Ramsar, estuarine crocodiles; Sundarbans — largest, Royal Bengal Tiger
### Offshore polymetallic nodule blocks near Great Nicobar
## 1. News
- Ministry of Mines notified 2 offshore blocks for composite licences (nodules and crusts) — ⚠ composite licence ≠ immediate mining
| Block | Area |
| West Sewell Ridge-01 (Andaman Sea) | 1,000 sq km |
| Sewell Rise-01 | 632 sq km |
- Combined 1,632 sq km; Great Nicobar also has a ₹72,000-crore project (transshipment port, airport, power plant, 2 cities)
## 2. Background
- Polymetallic nodules: nickel, cobalt, manganese, copper, REEs; ISA zones — Clarion–Clipperton (off Mexico), Central Indian Ocean Basin, Peru Basin
- Polymetallic sulphides: copper, zinc, lead, silver, gold
- ISA contracts: 2016 (Central Indian Ridge, Southwest Indian Ridge) and September 2025 — ⚠ India first country with two ISA sulphide contracts; executed by NCPOR, Goa
- EEZ — 200 nm (~370 km) under UNCLOS; seabed beyond = common heritage of mankind
- Offshore Areas Mineral (D&R) Act, 2002; India coastline 11,098 km; UN Ocean Decade 2021–2030
- Deep Ocean Mission: 2021, ₹4,077 crore (MoES)
- Sagar Manthan ocean research vessel — launched by GRSE on September 9 for NCPOR (89.5 m, 5,900 GT)
- Sagar Manthan (CA-360): ₹840 crore, built at Rishi Bankim Shipyard, Kolkata; operational 2028; surveys to 6 km depth; for equatorial Indian Ocean, Bay of Bengal, Antarctic waters
- NCPOR: est. 1998, Goa; Samudrayaan — manned deep-sea mission under Deep Ocean Mission (2021–2026)
### India's e-waste challenge and urban mining
## 1. Data
- E-waste generated: 13.98 lakh tonnes in 2024-25 (vs 12.54 lakh t in 2023-24)
- ⚠ India — 3rd-largest generator after China and USA
## 2. Rules
- E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022: in force 1 April 2023; 106 categories incl. solar PV
- EPR recycling target: 70% in 2025-26 and 2026-27; 80% from 2027-28
- Battery Waste Management Rules, 2022: ban landfill and incineration of waste batteries
- Hazardous and Other Wastes Rules, 2016; India party to Basel Convention
### Tso Kar Lake restoration
## 1. News
- Ladakh launched restoration of shrinking Tso Kar ("White Lake") on Rupshu Plateau
- Habitat of Black-necked Crane; Tso Kar Wetland Complex — Ramsar 2020 (Tso Kar + Startsapuk Tso)
- Tso Kar — high-altitude saline lake–wetland complex in southeastern Ladakh
### UNGA backs equal-area world maps
## 1. News
- UNGA resolution promoting equal-area maps — moved by Togo under "Correct the Map"; 164 of 193 in favour incl. India; ⚠ USA voted against
- Mercator projection (Gerardus Mercator, 1569) enlarges high latitudes — ⚠ Africa ~14 times larger than Greenland
- Mercator useful for navigation — constant compass bearings are straight lines; no flat map preserves area, shape, distance and direction together
- ⚠ Change is only visual — borders and sovereignty unaffected
### Anak Krakatau eruption
## 1. News
- Erupted September 6, 2026 in Sunda Strait (between Java and Sumatra) — flights hit at Jakarta's Soekarno–Hatta and Halim Perdanakusuma airports
- "Child of Krakatau" — rose from caldera of the 1883 Krakatau eruption; Indonesia on Pacific Ring of Fire
- Sunda Strait links Java Sea with Indian Ocean; 1883 eruption caused massive tsunamis
### Mandar of Jharkhand gets GI tag
## 1. News
- Mandar — traditional folk percussion instrument of Jharkhand — GI from Geographical Indications Registry, Chennai
- Double-sided barrel/cylindrical drum of red clay and leather; linked to tribal culture of Chotanagpur Plateau
- Main production centre: Jarjatta village, Raidih block, Gumla district
## 2. Background
- GI Act, 1999 — in force 2003; registry Chennai; validity 10 years, renewable; ⚠ India's first GI — Darjeeling Tea
### Kalpasar Project — India–Netherlands MoUs
## 1. News
- Two tripartite MoUs signed September 24, 2026 at the 9th India International Water Week, New Delhi
- Partners: Central Water Commission, Gujarat's Kalpasar Department, Deltares (Netherlands), Delft University of Technology
## 2. Project
- ~30-km dam across the Gulf of Khambhat (Gujarat) — freshwater reservoir; irrigation for 10 lakh+ ha, mainly Saurashtra
- CWC: est. 1945, New Delhi, Ministry of Jal Shakti
### Haryana's Parali Protection Force
## 1. News
- Haryana set up a Parali Protection Force before paddy harvest — field surveillance, drone monitoring, midnight patrols at district/block level
- Alternatives promoted: Happy Seeder, Super Seeder, mulching, composting, biomass use
- Crop-residue burning emits PM2.5, PM10, CO
- Haryana: formed November 1, 1966; capital Chandigarh

### Short-Lived Climate Pollutants (SLCPs)
## 1. Key facts
- SLCPs cause up to 45% of present warming; IPCC — rapid cuts can avoid up to 0.6°C by 2050
- Also called: super pollutants, Short-Lived Climate Forcers
- Major warming SLCPs: black carbon, methane, tropospheric ozone, HFCs
- ⚠ Black carbon is not a greenhouse gas — a particle that absorbs radiation; sulphate aerosols cool
- ⚠ Tropospheric ozone is not directly emitted — formed from methane, NOx, VOCs in sunlight
- Methane: ~80 times CO2 warming over 20 years; second-largest contributor after CO2
- Stratospheric ozone ~10–50 km; tropospheric surface–10 km
- Ozone causes loss of ~110 million tonnes staple crops/year; air pollution ~7 million premature deaths/year globally
## 2. India
- Air pollution: 1.67 million deaths in 2019 (17.8%), loss US$36.8 billion (1.36% of GDP)
- Methane (2016, Third BUR): Agriculture 73.96%, Waste 14.46%, Energy 10.62%, Industry 0.96%
- ⚠ India has not joined the Global Methane Pledge — calls farm methane "survival emissions"
## 3. International framework
| Instrument | Fact |
| Montreal Protocol | 1987 — ozone-depleting substances |
| Kigali Amendment | 2016; India ratified Sept 2021; freeze HFCs by 2028, cut 85% by 2047; can avoid 0.5°C by 2100 |
| Global Methane Pledge | COP26 Glasgow 2021; cut 30% from 2020 levels by 2030; 159 countries + EC |
| Climate and Clean Air Coalition | UNEP-led; India joined 2019 |
## 4. India's measures
- Air Act 1981; NAAQS 2009 (12 pollutants); National AQI 2015; GRAP 2017 (Delhi-NCR)
- NCAP: 130 cities, 24 States/UTs; 2025–26: 65 cities within PM2.5 limit 40 µg/m³; Delhi 102 µg/m³; only 14 met PM10 (60 µg/m³)
- PMUY: >10.5 crore LPG connections; GOBARdhan; SRI, DSR, alternate wetting & drying

### Nepal flash flood — rock–ice collapse
## 1. Event
- Date: August 26, 2026 — rock–ice collapse near Nepal–China border
- Toll: 579 deaths in Nepal; ~2,000 missing; 93,000+ need assistance; Kailash Mansarovar pilgrims missing
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say the toll was 1,259 (flash floods, Bhotekoshi-Trishuli rivers, Nepal, 26 Aug 2026)
- Initially read as a 4.4-magnitude quake; USGS said signal was from the collapse; energy equal to ~magnitude 5.2
- Material plunged ~1 km; ⚠ not a conventional GLOF — a rock–ice collapse-triggered debris flood
- Nepal: ~475 MW hydropower capacity lost
- 13 hydropower projects damaged (flash floods, Bhotekoshi-Trishuli rivers)
- China: Gyirong Port (Tibet) buried — upgraded at $175.8 million
- India: Rivers Gandak, Kosi, Ganga; Bihar and UP alert; Valmikinagar Barrage watched; Maharajganj, Kushinagar; India sent 47.5 tonnes of relief
## 2. India's Himalayan hazard measures
- National GLOF Risk Mitigation Programme: ₹150 crore — Arunachal Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand
- NDMA Guidelines on GLOFs: 2020; Dam Safety Act: 2021
- National Landslide Risk Mitigation Project: ₹1,000 crore, 15 States
### Pichavaram mangroves — economic valuation
## 1. Study
- Study: Economic Valuation and Blue Carbon Assessment of Ecosystem Services in the Pichavaram Mangrove Forest (Tamil Nadu)
- Total Economic Value: ₹2,485.38 crore (~₹1.83 crore/hectare)
- Survey of 302 households in 5 villages (MGR Thittu, Kalaignar Nagar, Chinnavaikal, Patriadi, Mudasalodai)
| Service | Value |
| Soil organic carbon | ₹1,224.63 crore (largest) |
| Carbon stock in vegetation | ₹387.77 crore |
| Oxygen regulation | ₹181.22 crore |
| Coastal protection | ₹147.17 crore |
- Vegetation carbon: 79.449 t/ha (~291.33 t CO₂/ha); soil organic carbon: 251.14 t/ha; combined carbon value ₹1,612.40 crore
- ⚠ Most mangrove carbon is below ground in sediments
## 2. Background
- ISFR 2023: mangrove cover 4,992 sq km (~0.15% of area; 4,628 in 2013); West Bengal > Gujarat > A&N Islands
- IUCN 2024: 50% of world's mangrove ecosystems at risk of collapse
- MISHTI (Mangrove Initiative for Shoreline Habitats and Tangible Incomes): launched 2023, ₹100 crore
- Adaptations: pneumatophores (breathing roots), prop roots, vivipary; mangroves called "Kidneys of the Planet"
- Bhitarkanika (Odisha) — Ramsar, estuarine crocodiles; Sundarbans — largest, Royal Bengal Tiger

## 2. Background

- Polymetallic sulphides: copper, zinc, lead, silver, gold
- ISA contracts: 2016 (Central Indian Ridge, Southwest Indian Ridge) and September 2025 — ⚠ India first country with two ISA sulphide contracts; executed by NCPOR, Goa
- EEZ — 200 nm (~370 km) under UNCLOS; seabed beyond = common heritage of mankind
- Offshore Areas Mineral (D&R) Act, 2002; India coastline 11,098 km; UN Ocean Decade 2021–2030
- Deep Ocean Mission: 2021, ₹4,077 crore (MoES)
- Sagar Manthan ocean research vessel — launched by GRSE on September 9 for NCPOR (89.5 m, 5,900 GT)
- Sagar Manthan (CA-360): ₹840 crore, built at Rishi Bankim Shipyard, Kolkata; operational 2028; surveys to 6 km depth; for equatorial Indian Ocean, Bay of Bengal, Antarctic waters
- NCPOR: est. 1998, Goa; Samudrayaan — manned deep-sea mission under Deep Ocean Mission (2021–2026)
### UNGA backs equal-area world maps
## 1. News
- UNGA resolution promoting equal-area maps — moved by Togo under "Correct the Map"; 164 of 193 in favour incl. India; ⚠ USA voted against
- Mercator projection (Gerardus Mercator, 1569) enlarges high latitudes — ⚠ Africa ~14 times larger than Greenland
- Mercator useful for navigation — constant compass bearings are straight lines; no flat map preserves area, shape, distance and direction together
- ⚠ Change is only visual — borders and sovereignty unaffected

### GI tags: India's GI ecosystem
## 1. Law and institutions
- Act: Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999; Rules 2002; operational 2003; registrations from 2004–05; Rules amended 2025
- International: Paris Convention, WTO TRIPS
- GI Registry: Chennai — all-India jurisdiction
- 2025 amendment: fees cut by 80%; renewal fee ₹3,000 to ₹500
- Validity: 10 years, renewable; revocation under Section 27
- Who can apply: association of producers, organisation or authority established by law
## 2. Key numbers
| Fact | Detail |
| First GI | Darjeeling Tea (West Bengal) |
| Authorised users | 365 to 29,000 in 10 years |
| Food products | 66 — e.g. Tirupati Laddu, Bardhaman Mihidana, Kendrapara Rasabali |
| Manufactured | 64 — Nashik Valley Wine, Kannauj Perfume |
| Natural | 5 — Makrana Marble, Chunar Balua Patthar, Lac of Purulia, Panna Diamond, Ambaji White Marble |
- Top States (Dec 2025): Uttar Pradesh 81, Tamil Nadu 76, Maharashtra 55
- Target: 10,000 GI registrations by 2030
- Income gain: 20–30% for artisans (Ministry of Textiles)
## 3. Examples and support
- Muga Silk Trail (Assam): announced June 2026 with a Silk Tourism Park
- MSME Innovative Scheme: up to 100% reimbursement, max ₹2 lakh per GI
| Market | GI product |
| Oman | Indi Lime (Karnataka), after India–Oman CEPA, Dec 2025 |
## 4. Bharat GI MoU
- MSME Ministry and DPIIT signed an MoU on 5 August 2026 at Vanijya Bhawan, New Delhi
- Bharat GI: GI producer collectives and authorised users onboarded to ONDC and GeM; linked with One District One Product
- GI Act administered by DPIIT (Ministry of Commerce); GeM: launched 2016; ONDC: by DPIIT
### Forest fires: Himalayan vulnerability
## 1. Parliamentary panel
- Committee: Parliamentary Standing Committee on Science and Technology, Environment, Forest and Climate Change
- Recommendations: geostationary satellite for 24x7 fire monitoring; regional climate-fire study; at least 2 NDRF centres for Himalayan fires; National Pine-Needle Utilisation Policy
- Himalayas: ~17% of landmass but ~57% of forest fire incidents
- Chir pine needles: "Pirul" — resin-rich, inflammable
## 2. Data and monitoring
- Forest and tree cover: ~8.27 lakh sq km (~24%)
- FSI vulnerability: 54.40% occasional fires; 7.49% moderately frequent; 2.40% high; 35.71% largely unaffected
- Season: November to June; peak March–May
- Satellites used now: foreign polar-orbiting AQUA and Suomi-NPP
- Types: ground, surface (common in India), crown (rare in India)
- Setting forests on fire punishable under BNS 2023; nodal ministry MoEFCC; National Action Plan on Forest Fire with Central Monitoring Committee
- Fire lines: kachha (undergrowth cleared), pucca (clear-felled strips)
## 3. Background — NDMA
- NDMA: under Disaster Management Act 2005 (23 December 2005); chaired by Prime Minister; SDMA by Chief Minister
- NDRF: National Disaster Response Force — under MHA
### Small River Rejuvenation Framework (NMCG)
## 1. Framework
- Draft unveiled under National Mission for Clean Ganga; nature-based, low-cost restoration
- Small river: ~50–150 km, within 1–2 districts
- Interventions: pollution and climate; connectivity (wetlands, floodplains); geomorphological correction
- Funding by convergence: VB-G RAM G, Swachh Bharat Mission, Namami Gange
## 2. Degraded rivers
| River | City / note |
| Sahibi | Yamuna tributary — Najafgarh drain, Delhi |
| Mithi | Mumbai (from Vihar Lake) |
| Musi | Hyderabad; Krishna tributary; aka Muchukunda |
| Cooum | Chennai — once "Thames of South India" |
| Vrishabhavathi | Bengaluru |
| Bharalu, Bahini | Guwahati |
| Varuna, Assi | Varanasi |
| Mula, Mutha | Pune |
- UP: One District–One River — Noon, Sakarni, Ghurari, Manorama, Tamsa; Kerala: Chithari, Kammalamkudi
## 3. Inland waterways
- Cost per tonne-km: waterways ₹1.06, rail ₹1.36, road ₹2.50
- Freight share: waterways ~2%, rail ~18%, road ~71%
- National Waterways: 111 (Act 2016 — 5 existing + 106 new) in 23 States, 4 UTs; 32 operational; Budget 2026–27 — 20 more in 5 years
- IWAI Act 1986; Inland Vessels Act 2021; Coastal Shipping Act 2025; River Cruise Tourism Roadmap 2047 (2024)
### Parichha Weir — ICID World Heritage Irrigation Structure
## 1. News
- Recognised: 13 August 2026 by International Commission on Irrigation and Drainage (ICID)
- Where: Jhansi, UP, on Betwa River (Betwa Canal System) — ~140 years old; irrigates UP and MP
- ICID: est. 1950, HQ New Delhi
- Operational since: 1886; ⚠ second such structure in the region after Sukwa–Dukwan Weir (recognised 2022)
- History: canal proposed by Captain Strachey (1855); construction from 1881, ~₹40 lakh

| September 2025 | Gokul Reservoir (Buxar), Udaipur Jheel (West Champaran), Bihar |
| 5 June 2025 | Menar (Udaipur), Khichan (Phalodi), Rajasthan |
| 2 February 2025 | Sakkarakottai, Therthangal (TN); Khecheopalri (Sikkim); Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) |
## 2. Key facts
- Total: 101 sites, ~13.88 lakh ha; 26 (2012) to 100 (June 2026)
- Count: 98 early 2026 → Shekha Jheel (UP) 99th (Apr) → Surha Tal / JP Narayan Bird Sanctuary (Ballia, UP) 100th (5 Jun 2026) → Glaw Lake (Arunachal) 101st (3 Aug 2026), in Kamlang TR, Arunachal's first
- Rank: 1st in Asia, 3rd globally
- First sites: Chilika (Odisha), Keoladeo (Rajasthan)
- Most: Tamil Nadu 20, Uttar Pradesh 13
- Largest: Sundarban Wetland; smallest: Vembannur Wetland Complex (TN) (earlier: Renuka Wetland, HP)
- Convention: 1971, Ramsar (Iran); in force 1975; India joined 1 February 1982
## 3. Latest sites (one-liners)
- 101st: Glaw Lake, in Kamlang Tiger Reserve and Wildlife Sanctuary — ⚠ Arunachal Pradesh's first Ramsar site
- 100th: Surha Tal (Jayaprakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary), Ballia, UP; 99th: Shekha Jheel (UP)
- ⚠ 99th = Shekha Jheel; 100th = Surha Tal; 101st = Glaw Lake

### Carbosulfan ban proposed
## 1. Draft notification
- Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare proposed a complete ban on carbosulfan — manufacture, import, transport, distribution, sale and use
- Why: highly hazardous carbamate insecticide; converts to highly toxic carbofuran — harms birds, pollinators, aquatic life
- After consulting: Registration Committee under the Insecticides Act, 1968
## 2. Background
- Insecticides Act 1968: regulates import, manufacture, sale, transport, distribution, use
- Central Insecticides Board and Registration Committee: Ministry of Agriculture

| Maharashtra | 17,340 |
| Kerala | 9,993.7 |

### International Tiger Day and India's tiger model
## 1. Key facts
- International Tiger Day: 29 July — set at Saint Petersburg Tiger Summit, 2010 (13 range countries); Tx2 Goal — double wild tigers by 2022
- India has about 70% of world's wild tigers; AITE 2022: 3,682
- Tiger: Panthera tigris, family Felidae, IUCN Endangered; national animal — Royal Bengal Tiger
## 2. Framework
- Project Tiger — MoEFCC flagship; NTCA oversees; Tiger Conservation Plans under WPA 1972; WCCB against wildlife crime
- Core (Critical Tiger Habitat), buffer, corridors
- All India Tiger Estimation: every four years — NTCA + Wildlife Institute of India
- M-STrIPES: Monitoring System for Tigers – Intensive Protection and Ecological Status (GPS, GPRS, remote sensing)
- International Big Cat Alliance: seven big cats — tiger, lion, leopard, snow leopard, cheetah, jaguar, puma; Secretariat in India
- Global Tiger Forum: HQ New Delhi — only intergovernmental body for tigers
- CA|TS: Conservation Assured Tiger Standards
- ⚠ Largest Critical Tiger Habitat (PYQ): Nagarjunasagar-Srisailam (AP/Telangana)
### El Niño and weak monsoon — economic risk
## 1. What happened
- First month of monsoon: rainfall deficit of about 40%
- IMD forecast: below-normal rainfall in July (below 94% of normal)
- Reservoirs (166, CWC): 47.725 BCM on July 2 vs 78.077 BCM last year
- Agri exports: CAGR 8.2% (2020–2025), about 12% of core exports
- Agriculture: about one-fifth of GVA; 46% of workforce; 55% of population linked
- El Niño drought years: 1972, 1982, 2009, 2015
## 2. Background
- El Niño: warming of central-eastern equatorial Pacific; Spanish for "Little Boy / Christ Child"; weakens trade winds, weakens Humboldt (Peruvian) Current and upwelling
- ENSO phases: El Niño, La Niña, Neutral; ⚠ La Niña generally strengthens Indian monsoon
- IMD: est. 1875; Ministry of Earth Sciences; HQ New Delhi; 6 Regional Meteorological Centres — Mumbai, Chennai, New Delhi, Kolkata, Nagpur, Guwahati; one of six WMO RSMCs — names cyclones in North Indian Ocean
### Glacial lake expansion and GLOF risk — Mago Chu Basin
## 1. What happened
- Study: Mago Chu Basin, Tawang district, Arunachal Pradesh — 4 of 5 glacial lakes expanded between 2016 and 2026, including high and very high risk lakes
## 2. Types of glacial lakes (ICIMOD)
| Type | Note / Example |
| Glacial erosion | Cirque and valley lakes |
| Moraine-dammed | ⚠ Most vulnerable to GLOF (loose debris) — Samudra Tapu, Gepang Gath (HP) |
- Glacial Lake Atlas 2023: by NRSC (ISRO) — Brahmaputra basin has highest concentration
- Elevation-dependent warming: high altitudes warm faster
## 3. Events and mitigation
- Kedarnath 2013 — Chorabari lake; South Lhonak Lake, Sikkim, 2023
- NDMA GLOF Guidelines: 2020; CWC risk indexing of lakes
- National GLOF Risk Mitigation Project (NGRMP): launched 2025 — Arunachal Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand
- Siachen Glacier: north of Nubra Valley
### Flash floods and cloudbursts — Assam floods
## 1. What happened
- Assam floods first blamed on cloudburst in Nagaland; IMD said it was heavy rainfall, not cloudburst threshold
- IMD cloudburst definition: more than 100 mm in one hour over 20–30 sq km
- Flash flood: develops in less than six hours
- ⚠ Not every flash flood is a cloudburst (dam release, GLOF, saturated soil, poor drainage)
- 1°C warming → about 7% more atmospheric moisture
- Flash floods most frequent in Brahmaputra basin, then Ganga and Krishna
## 2. NDMA background
- Disaster Management Act, 2005; NDMA constituted 27 September 2006; chaired by PM; PM + 9 members
- High Powered Committee 1999; Gujarat earthquake 2001
- SDMA — CM; DDMA — District Collector; supervises NIDM
### White-bellied heron and Lohit hydropower project
## 1. What happened
- Proposed hydropower project in Lohit river basin, Arunachal Pradesh threatens the white-bellied heron
- ⚠ Project EIA did not include the heron
- Lohit basin (incl. Lahm tributary) holds about 70% of India's white-bellied herons; also Noa-Dehing river
## 2. Species
| Aspect | Detail |
| IUCN | Critically Endangered |
| WPA 1972 | Schedule I |
| Habitat | Fast-flowing Himalayan rivers, mature riparian forests |
- First described 1878 from Teesta region — no longer found there; Bhutan has captive breeding
## 3. IUCN Red List background
- Criterion D: CR < 50, EN < 250, VU < 1,000 mature individuals
- Criterion E: CR if extinction probability ≥ 50% in 10 years / 3 generations
- ISSG (Global Invasive Species Database) belongs to IUCN (SSC), est. 1994

## 1. News

## 2. Summer vs winter

## 3. Key concepts
- PM10: diameter 10 micrometres or less
- Loo: hot long-distance dust winds (North India); Andhi: short local dust storm with thunderstorms

### Biochar — farm waste to black gold
## 1. Facts
- Production: pyrolysis — oxygen-depleted heating at 300–700°C; by-products syngas and bio-oil
- ⚠ Decomposes very slowly (stores carbon for centuries)
- Benefits: water-holding +10–25%; yield +10–30%; 1 t biochar sequesters 2.5–3 t CO2e
- Carbon credits: VM0042 methodology — 2–2.8 t CO2e credits per tonne; KISAN kiln by IIT Kharagpur
- India: crop waste 500 MT+/yr; Punjab–Haryana burn 20 MT+ paddy straw; MSW 62 MT (50%+ biodegradable)
- Potential: syngas 20–30 MT (8–13 TWh); bio-oil 24–40 MT (substitutes 12–19 MT diesel/kerosene)
- Concrete: 2–5% biochar — heat resistance +20%, captures ~115 kg CO2/m³
- Trials: Akola (maize stalk, black soil); Kerala (coconut leaf stalk); abroad — Kenya (rice husk), Thailand, Brazil (Embrapa)
- PYQ (2020): biochar in vertical farming — aids N-fixing microbes, retains water (all correct)
### Europe's 2026 heatwave — Omega Block
## 1. Facts
- Cause: Omega Block — high pressure between two lows, shaped like Ω; stalls the jet stream (west-to-east)
- Heat dome: sinking, compressed air under the high; ⚠ suppresses clouds and rain
- Europe: warming at more than twice the global rate — fastest-warming continent
- Concepts: climate whiplash (rapid swing between extremes); albedo loss from Arctic ice; tropical night; urban heat island
- El Niño: developing — raises probability (⚠ not certainty) of weak Indian monsoon
### Tunnels, passes and rivers in news
## 1. Tunnels and passes
| Item | Fact |
| Zojila Tunnel | 13.153 km; J&K–Ladakh; world's highest road tunnel under construction; India's longest bi-directional single-tube road tunnel; 11,578 ft, Baltal–Minamarg; built by MEIL for NHIDCL |
| Photu La Tunnel | Approved on Srinagar–Leh NH-01; ₹1,196.22 crore; 2.65 km (1.96 km twin-tube + 0.69 km approach roads); cuts 8.5 km; Photu La — highest pass on the highway (~4,108 m) |
| Lipulekh Pass | India–China border trade resumed after 6 years (suspended 2020, COVID-19); near India–Nepal–China tri-junction; Kailash–Manasarovar route; new customs office at Gunji; links Pithoragarh with Purang (Taklakot); ~5,200 m; ⚠ first India–China trading point opened (1992); others — Shipki La (HP), Nathu La (Sikkim) |
## 2. Rivers and water disputes
- Mekedatu: Tamil Nadu Assembly resolution against Karnataka's dam on Cauvery; CWDT 1990, award 2007; SC judgment 2018; CWMA 2018
- Kiru (624 MW): 83% complete, target December 2026; between Kirthai-II and Kwar projects; by CVPPPL (NHPC + JKSPDC JV)
- Tribunals extended by one year: Ravi–Beas Tribunal (1986 — Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan) and KWDT-II (2004 — AP, Telangana, Karnataka, Maharashtra), under ISRWD Act, 1956
- Chenab: from Chandra + Bhaga in Himachal; Krishna rises at Mahabaleshwar
- Aravalli: SC formed expert committee chaired by Kanchan Devi to define its limits — report by 31 August 2026; one of world's oldest fold mountains (Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat); highest peak Guru Shikhar (1,722 m)
- Kishau Multipurpose Dam: six States — Himachal, Uttarakhand, Delhi, UP, Haryana, Rajasthan — signed MoU; ₹15,000 crore, on Tons River (⚠ largest tributary of Yamuna); Centre bears 90% of water component, States 10%
- Chenab–Beas Link Tunnel: approved, ₹2,352 crore — 8.7-km tunnel in Himachal Pradesh to divert surplus Chenab water to Beas; by NHPC; ~4,000 MW extra hydropower; Indus Waters Treaty 1960 (World Bank)
### Volcanoes, earthquakes and global conservation
## 1. Disasters
- Volcán de Fuego (Guatemala): ash up to 33,000 ft
- Mount Merapi (Java, near Yogyakarta, Indonesia) erupted — stratovolcano; Indo-Australian Plate subducting under Sunda Plate; Indonesia has most active volcanoes (120+)
- Kīlauea (Hawaii, USA): shield volcano of the Hawaiian hotspot, basaltic lava; in Hawaiʻi Volcanoes NP; monitored by USGS
## 2. Conservation
- Lakshadweep: giant Pavona clavus (Potato Patch) coral colony near Kadmat Island — 4,250 sq m, 700–1,800 years old; age via sclerochronology; India's only coral atoll archipelago
- Agasthyamalai Biosphere Reserve (TN–Kerala): SC ordered removal of encroachments — action plan in 1 month, eviction in 6 months; UNESCO MAB 2016
- Niokolo-Koba NP (Senegal): GEF USD 1.2587 million via IUCN (est. 1948, Gland); ~9.13 lakh ha, 70+ mammal and 329 bird species; WHS + Biosphere Reserve; game reserve 1925, NP 1954, WHS 1981, off Danger List 2024
- Berijam Lake (Kodaikanal, Upper Palani Hills, Dindigul): artificial drinking-water reservoir; Madras HC Madurai Bench sent petition for tiger reserve feasibility study to the Forest Bench
- SHAN Conservation Foundation: approved by Ladakh LG for snow leopards (*Panthera uncia*; IUCN Vulnerable; WPA Schedule I) — under Ladakh Dept of Environment and Forests; Project Snow Leopard 2009
- IBCA: Bangladesh became 27th member of International Big Cat Alliance (launched by PM on 9 April 2023 at Mysuru) — 7 big cats: tiger, lion, leopard, snow leopard, cheetah, jaguar, puma

## Forest fires
- Himalayas — about 57% of forest-fire incidents; about 17% of landmass

- Moraine-dammed lakes (ICIMOD) — most GLOF-prone (loose debris)
- Cloudburst (IMD) — over 100 mm in one hour over 20–30 sq km
## ENSO
- El Niño — warming of central-eastern equatorial Pacific; weakens trade winds, Humboldt Current, upwelling
- Developing El Niño — raises the probability of a weak monsoon (not certain)
- La Niña — generally strengthens the Indian monsoon; ENSO phases: El Niño, La Niña, Neutral
## Weather events
- 2026 European heatwave — Omega Block (high between two lows, stalls jet stream)
- Heat dome — sinking, compressed air stops clouds and rain
- Typhoon Bavi — alerts in Japan's Sakishima Islands and Taiwan

### Forest fires: Himalayan vulnerability
## 1. Parliamentary panel
- Committee: Parliamentary Standing Committee on Science and Technology, Environment, Forest and Climate Change
- Recommendations: geostationary satellite for 24x7 fire monitoring; regional climate-fire study; at least 2 NDRF centres for Himalayan fires; National Pine-Needle Utilisation Policy
- Himalayas: ~17% of landmass but ~57% of forest fire incidents
- Chir pine needles: "Pirul" — resin-rich, inflammable
## 2. Data and monitoring
- Forest and tree cover: ~8.27 lakh sq km (~24%)
- FSI vulnerability: 54.40% occasional fires; 7.49% moderately frequent; 2.40% high; 35.71% largely unaffected
- Season: November to June; peak March–May
- Satellites used now: foreign polar-orbiting AQUA and Suomi-NPP
- Types: ground, surface (common in India), crown (rare in India)
- Setting forests on fire punishable under BNS 2023; nodal ministry MoEFCC; National Action Plan on Forest Fire with Central Monitoring Committee
- Fire lines: kachha (undergrowth cleared), pucca (clear-felled strips)
## 3. Background — NDMA
- NDMA: under Disaster Management Act 2005 (23 December 2005); chaired by Prime Minister; SDMA by Chief Minister
- NDRF: National Disaster Response Force — under MHA

### Flash floods and cloudbursts — Assam floods
## 1. What happened
- Assam floods first blamed on cloudburst in Nagaland; IMD said it was heavy rainfall, not cloudburst threshold
- IMD cloudburst definition: more than 100 mm in one hour over 20–30 sq km
- Flash flood: develops in less than six hours
- ⚠ Not every flash flood is a cloudburst (dam release, GLOF, saturated soil, poor drainage)
- 1°C warming → about 7% more atmospheric moisture
- Flash floods most frequent in Brahmaputra basin, then Ganga and Krishna
## 2. NDMA background
- Disaster Management Act, 2005; NDMA constituted 27 September 2006; chaired by PM; PM + 9 members
- High Powered Committee 1999; Gujarat earthquake 2001
- SDMA — CM; DDMA — District Collector; supervises NIDM
### White-bellied heron and Lohit hydropower project
## 1. What happened
- Proposed hydropower project in Lohit river basin, Arunachal Pradesh threatens the white-bellied heron
- ⚠ Project EIA did not include the heron
- Lohit basin (incl. Lahm tributary) holds about 70% of India's white-bellied herons; also Noa-Dehing river
## 2. Species
| Aspect | Detail |
| IUCN | Critically Endangered |
| WPA 1972 | Schedule I |
| Habitat | Fast-flowing Himalayan rivers, mature riparian forests |
- First described 1878 from Teesta region — no longer found there; Bhutan has captive breeding
## 3. IUCN Red List background
- Criterion D: CR < 50, EN < 250, VU < 1,000 mature individuals
- Criterion E: CR if extinction probability ≥ 50% in 10 years / 3 generations
- ISSG (Global Invasive Species Database) belongs to IUCN (SSC), est. 1994
### New GI tags — July 2026
- GI tag — an intellectual property right for products whose quality/reputation comes from geographical origin
### Van Mahotsav and India State of Forest Report
## 1. Facts
- 77th Van Mahotsav: July 1–7, 2026; started 1950 by K. M. Munshi (then Union Minister for Food and Agriculture)
- ISFR 2023: forest + tree cover 8,27,357 sq km = 25.17% of area; up 1,445 sq km over 2021
- ISFR: biennial, by Forest Survey of India (est. 1981, Dehradun)

### Summer air pollution in Indian cities
## 1. News
- CAQM reimposed GRAP Stage-I in Delhi in May 2026 — summer pollution episode
## 2. Summer vs winter
| Feature | Summer | Winter |
| Peak time | Midday / afternoon | Early morning / night (inversion) |
| Driver | Heat, sunlight, dust storms | Cold, fog, stagnant air, stubble burning |
## 3. Key concepts
- PM10: diameter 10 micrometres or less
- Loo: hot long-distance dust winds (North India); Andhi: short local dust storm with thunderstorms
- AQEWS (Delhi): 3-day AQI forecasts; extended to Jaipur, Mumbai
- Mumbai AQDSS: BMC with CEEW — action on 1,000+ construction sites since October 2025
- PYQ: cloud seeding uses silver iodide and potassium iodide
### Great Nicobar Project — security vs ecology
## 1. Project components
- International Container Transshipment Terminal at Galathea Bay; greenfield international airport; township; power plant
- Location: southernmost island of A&N; near Six Degree Channel and Malacca Strait
- Aim: cut dependence on foreign hubs — Colombo, Singapore, Port Klang
## 2. Concerns
- Giant Leatherback Turtle nesting at Galathea Bay
- Shompen — PVTG; also Nicobarese community
- Lakhs of trees felled; coral reefs; high seismic / tsunami zone (2004 tsunami)
- NGT refused to interfere with environmental clearance, demanding strict compliance
## 3. Background
- Andaman and Nicobar Command: India's only tri-service command
- PYQ: Barren Island — India's active volcano; Ten Degree Channel separates Andaman and Nicobar
### Jai Prakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary (Surha Tal) — India's 100th Ramsar site
- Surha Tal (Jai Prakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary) — India's 100th Ramsar site; Ballia, UP; UP's 13th
### Biochar — farm waste to black gold
## 1. Facts
- Production: pyrolysis — oxygen-depleted heating at 300–700°C; by-products syngas and bio-oil
- ⚠ Decomposes very slowly (stores carbon for centuries)
- Benefits: water-holding +10–25%; yield +10–30%; 1 t biochar sequesters 2.5–3 t CO2e
- Carbon credits: VM0042 methodology — 2–2.8 t CO2e credits per tonne; KISAN kiln by IIT Kharagpur
- India: crop waste 500 MT+/yr; Punjab–Haryana burn 20 MT+ paddy straw; MSW 62 MT (50%+ biodegradable)
- Potential: syngas 20–30 MT (8–13 TWh); bio-oil 24–40 MT (substitutes 12–19 MT diesel/kerosene)
- Concrete: 2–5% biochar — heat resistance +20%, captures ~115 kg CO2/m³
- Trials: Akola (maize stalk, black soil); Kerala (coconut leaf stalk); abroad — Kenya (rice husk), Thailand, Brazil (Embrapa)
- PYQ (2020): biochar in vertical farming — aids N-fixing microbes, retains water (all correct)
### Tunnels, passes and rivers in news
- BRO — 1960; HQ New Delhi; under Ministry of Defence
### Volcanoes, earthquakes and global conservation

### Updated NDC (2035)
- India's new NDC: 60% non-fossil capacity and 47% lower emissions intensity by 2035
| Parameter | 2022 NDC | New NDC |
| Non-fossil capacity | 50% by 2030 (met 2025) | 60% by 2035 |
| Emissions intensity cut (vs 2005) | 45% (⚠ other notes: 44%) | 47% |
| Carbon sink | 2.5-3 bn t | 3.5-4 bn t CO₂e |
| Long-term | Net zero 2070 | Uses intensity, not absolute |

## 2. Background
- IBCA launched: by PM Modi on 9 April 2023 — 50 years of Project Tiger

## 3. Saudi Arabia joins IBCA

### Climate terms — Western Disturbance, ENSO, heatwave
## 1. News
- IMD forecast fresh Western Disturbance over North India 11–13 May
- US Climate Prediction Center (April 2026): ENSO-neutral now; El Niño likely in May–July 2026 — 61% chance, persisting to end-2026
## 2. Terms
- Western Disturbance: east-moving rain system from beyond Afghanistan/Iran; moisture from Mediterranean, Black, Caspian, Arabian seas; helps wheat
- ENSO phases: El Niño (warm), La Niña (cool), Neutral
| IMD heatwave criterion | Value |
| Minimum max temp | 40°C plains, 30°C hills |
| Heat wave (departure) | 4.5–6.4°C above normal |
| Severe heat wave (departure) | over 6.4°C |
| Heat wave (absolute) | 45°C |
| Severe heat wave (absolute) | 47°C |
- Wet bulb temperature: above 35°C sustained can be fatal
- Feels-like (apparent) temperature: temp + humidity + wind
### Solid Waste Management Rules, 2026
## 1. Facts
- In force: 1 April 2026, replacing SWM Rules 2016
- Aims: source segregation, bulk generators, scientific processing, landfill reduction, legacy dumpsite remediation, circular economy, digital monitoring (reporting to CPCB)
## 2. Background
- Environment Protection Act, 1986 enacted under Article 253 (treaty implementation) — after Stockholm Declaration 1972
- Subsidiarity: functions at lowest capable level

## 1. Key facts

- Freshwater wetland; 180+ migratory bird species in winter; on Central Asian Flyway
- India's Ramsar sites: 26 (2014) → 99 (2026) — nearing 100
| Batch | Sites |
| 2026 (to 98) | Patna Bird Sanctuary (UP, as per source), Chhari-Dhand (Gujarat) |
| 2025 | Siliserh Lake (Rajasthan), Kopra Reservoir (Chhattisgarh), Goga Beel, Gokul Reservoir, Udaipur Jheel (Bihar), Menar, Khichan (Rajasthan) |
| World Wetlands Day | Sakkarakottai, Thirthangal (TN), Khecheopalri (Sikkim), Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) |

- First Indian Ramsar sites: Chilika Lake and Keoladeo NP; most sites — Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh
- Largest — Sundarbans; smallest — Vembannur Wetland Complex (TN; earlier: Renuka, HP)

### Tiger "Explorer" from Tadoba returns to East Godavari
## 1. News
- Adult male tiger "Explorer" from Tadoba-Andhari Tiger Reserve (Maharashtra) re-entered East Godavari after crossing the Godavari

- East & West Godavari; ~1,012.86 sq km (CARE; a daily page says 86 sq km); Papikondalu hills, Eastern Ghats; both banks of Godavari
- Reserved Forest 1882 → Wildlife Sanctuary 1978 → National Park 2008; tropical moist deciduous; tiger, gaur, mouse deer
- ⚠ Papikonda is a National Park, not a Tiger Reserve
- Tadoba-Andhari: Chandrapur district; named after Tadoba Lake and Andhari River

### IBCA "New Delhi Declaration" for big cat summit
## 1. News
- International Big Cat Alliance (IBCA) preparing "New Delhi Declaration" for global big cat summit likely on June 1, 2026 — first international declaration exclusively on big cats
- Focus: habitat connectivity, wildlife crime, finance, science, community participation, One Health
## 2. IBCA facts
- Launched by PM Narendra Modi on 9 April 2023 — 50 years of Project Tiger
- Treaty-based organisation from 23 January 2025; ⚠ HQ/secretariat in India (not Geneva)
- 24 member countries (2026); observers incl. Kazakhstan, Namibia, Thailand
- Seven big cats: tiger, lion, leopard, snow leopard, cheetah, jaguar, puma (⚠ no rhinoceros); India has five
## 3. India's role
- ~75% of world's wild tigers; 50+ Tiger Reserves; NTCA under MoEFCC; Project Tiger 1973
- Asiatic lion only in Gir; snow leopard ~10–15% of global population — ⚠ Vulnerable (downlisted 2017)
- Project Cheetah (2022) — Kuno NP (MP); cheetah declared extinct in India 1952
- IUCN: 1948, Gland, Switzerland — ⚠ not a UN agency; Red List informs CITES; WWF + IUCN created TRAFFIC
### River Basin Management Scheme extended to 2030–31
## 1. News
- RBM Scheme continued for 2026–27 to 2030–31 — outlay ₹2,183 crore (previous cycle ₹1,276 crore); Ministry of Jal Shakti
- Treats whole river system (tributaries, floodplains, groundwater, wetlands) as one hydrological unit
- India: ~4% of world freshwater, ~17% of population
## 2. Features
- Flood/erosion control in NE — e.g. Majuli Island (Assam); springshed management in hills
## 3. Institutions
| Body | Role |
| Central Water Commission (CWC) | Planning, flood forecasting |
| National Water Development Agency (NWDA) | Interlinking of Rivers — e.g. Ken-Betwa Link |
| Brahmaputra Board | Flood, erosion in Brahmaputra and Barak valleys (⚠ not coastal erosion) |
- Brahmaputra: Yarlung Tsangpo in Tibet; Great Bend near Namcha Barwa; enters India as Siang/Dihang (Arunachal); higher discharge/sediment than Indus
- ⚠ Fully centrally funded; basins covered include Brahmaputra, Barak, Teesta, Indus; Indus Waters Treaty 1960; Ministry of Jal Shakti formed 2019
### Great Indian Bustard chick via "jumpstart method" goes missing in Kutch
## 1. News
- GIB chick hatched March 26, 2026 at Naiya grassland, Kutch (Gujarat) — first successful jumpstart method use in Gujarat; flew ~April 18, then missing 4–5 days (likely predation); not geotagged
- Jumpstart: infertile egg of wild female replaced with fertile egg from Rajasthan captive centre; wild mother raises chick
- Gujarat has only three surviving wild female GIBs
## 2. GIB facts
- Ardeotis nigriceps; IUCN Critically Endangered; WPA Schedule I; CITES Appendix I; CMS
- State bird of Rajasthan; arid/semi-arid grasslands; global population < 200
- Biggest threat: power line collisions; lays one egg at a time
- Captive breeding: Ramdevra and Sam (Jaisalmer); Species Recovery Programme under IDWH (100% central assistance), with WII
## 3. Related species status

| White-bellied Heron | Critically Endangered |
| Himalayan Quail | Critically Endangered |
| Asiatic Lion | Endangered |

### Solid Waste Management Rules 2026 in force
## 1. Key facts
- Effective April 1, 2026, replacing 2016 rules — under Environment Protection Act, 1986; goal zero landfill, circular economy
- Four-way segregation: wet, dry, sanitary, special domestic hazardous
- Bulk generators: > 100 kg/day waste, > 40,000 litres water use, or > 20,000 sq m area — on-site processing
- Industries raise RDF use 5% → 15% over six years; biomining/bioremediation for legacy dumps; tourist user charges in hill/island areas
- Related: Plastic Waste Rules 2016, E-Waste Rules 2022, Biomedical Waste Rules 2016; MoEFCC est. 1985; FSI — 1981, Dehradun, DG Anoop Singh
### Climate change reshaping disease patterns in India
## 1. Key findings
- ~40% of districts at high risk from extreme weather
- Dengue spreading to non-traditional areas — Shimla, parts of J&K
- Climate change = "health-risk multiplier"
## 2. Policy response
| Initiative | Key facts |
| NPCCHH | National Programme on Climate Change and Human Health — 2018, MoHFW |
| NAPCC | 2008; 8 national missions |
## 3. Related static GK
- Global Stocktake (Paris Agreement) — every five years, reviews collective progress (no targets/penalties)
- 1.5°C measured against pre-industrial (1850–1900) levels
- Blue carbon — mangroves, seagrass, salt marshes; mangroves store 3–5 times more carbon per ha than tropical forests
### Climate adaptation — Tamil Nadu Climate Resilient Villages model
## 1. News
- Economic Survey 2025–26 highlighted Tamil Nadu's Climate Resilient Villages (CRV) Programme — 11 vulnerable districts; supported by WRI India
- India: ~$170 billion losses from 430 extreme events (1995–2024), ~1.3 billion affected; ranks 9th in climate risk (Germanwatch Climate Risk Index)
- Climate Risk Index 2025 (COP30), Germanwatch: India 9th worst hit (1995-2024); India 15th for 2024 alone (APPCA 2026); India "9th most climate-vulnerable" (TH, 24 Apr 2026)
## 2. Key facts
- UNEP Adaptation Gap Report 2025: developing countries' annual gap $284–339 billion up to 2035; ⚠ report is on adaptation (not Article 6 carbon markets)
- India's adaptation spending ~5.6% of GDP (FY22); WRI — every rupee gives ~10 times return
- NICRA — National Innovations in Climate Resilient Agriculture — ⚠ by ICAR (not MoEFCC); 651 districts
- COP30 emphasised Locally Led Adaptation (LLA)
- Paris Agreement: COP21 (2015); well below 2°C, pursue 1.5°C; NDCs voluntary; Global Goal on Adaptation
### Earthquake Lights (EQL)
## 1. Key facts
- Luminous atmospheric phenomena associated with seismic activity — may appear before or during earthquakes
- Cause: ionisation of air by electrical charges generated under tectonic stress in rocks
### Improved biomass cookstoves vs LPG
## 1. Key facts
- Improved Cookstoves (ICS): secondary aeration → fuller combustion
- Thermal efficiency: traditional chulha ~10%; ICS 38–45%; fuel use cut up to two-thirds
- Cost: household ICS < ₹2,000; commercial up to ₹20,000; eligible for carbon credits
- ⚠ ICS reduce but do not eliminate indoor pollution; reduce black carbon
## 2. Pollutant facts
- Photochemical smog: NOx + VOCs in sunlight → ozone, PAN
- Secondary pollutants: ozone, PAN; ⚠ CO is primary
### Rare earth mining pollutes Mekong tributaries
## 1. News
- Unregulated rare earth mining in Myanmar and Laos — heavy metals (arsenic, mercury, lead, cadmium) in tributaries Sai and Ruak → Mekong; threat to "world's kitchen"
## 2. Mekong facts
- Longest river of Southeast Asia; rises in Tibetan Plateau as Lancang; flows through China, Myanmar, Laos, Thailand, Cambodia, Vietnam → South China Sea
- Mekong Delta (Vietnam) — "rice bowl" of SE Asia; Tonle Sap Lake (Cambodia) — seasonal flow reversal
- Mekong River Commission (1995): ⚠ four full members — Cambodia, Laos, Thailand, Vietnam; China, Myanmar = dialogue partners
## 3. Rare earth facts
- 17 elements — lanthanides + scandium + yttrium
- Lanthanum — optical glass; Dysprosium — NdFeB magnets (EV motors)
- Monazite contains thorium, uranium; ionic clay deposits — in-situ leaching; ⚠ froth flotation cannot separate individual REEs
### Yana Caves declared national Geo-heritage site
## 1. Key facts
- Yana Caves near Gokarna, Uttara Kannada (Karnataka) — declared by Geological Survey of India on April 13, 2026 (176th GSI foundation day)
- ~2,700 million years old; Western Ghats; tectonic + karst processes
### Varkala in UN "20 Cities Towards Zero Waste"
## 1. Key facts
- Varkala (Kerala) in first list of "20 Cities Towards Zero Waste" — led by UN Secretary-General's Advisory Board on Zero Waste (launched 2023); supported by UN-Habitat and UNEP
- UN-Habitat (1978) and UNEP (1972) — both HQ Nairobi
### Bhavsagar Referral Centre — National Repository for Deep Sea Fauna
## 1. Key facts
- Bhavsagar Referral Centre (of CMLRE, Kochi — est. 1998, Ministry of Earth Sciences) designated National Repository for Deep Sea Fauna by MoEFCC under Biological Diversity Act, 2002; custodian of type specimens, DNA data
- 3,500+ geo-referenced specimens — cnidarians, annelids, molluscs, arthropods, echinoderms, elasmobranchs, teleost fishes; aligned with UN Decade of Ocean Science (2021–2030)
- NBA: est. 2003, Chennai

- May weaken India's southwest monsoon; reduces Atlantic hurricanes, raises Pacific activity

- New: Changshan, Mt. Siguniang (China), Terres d'Hérault (France), Nisyros (Greece), Joyce Country and Western Lakes (Ireland), Mine-Akiyoshidai Karst Plateau (Japan), Lenggong, Sarawak Delta (Malaysia), Algarvensis (Portugal), Toratau (Russia), Dahar (Tunisia), Manantiales Serranos (Uruguay)
- Recognition valid 4 years; UNESCO — 1945, Paris
### India's updated NDC for 2035
## 1. News
- Union Cabinet approved India's updated NDC, to be communicated to the UNFCCC
- Approval: Cabinet, 25 Mar 2026 (another note: Cabinet, Apr 2026); India's updated NDC for 2031-35
- ⚠ India and Argentina were the only G20 countries without a 2035 NDC by end-2025
| Parameter | 2022 NDC | New NDC (2035) |
| Non-fossil installed capacity | 50% by 2030 | 60% by 2035 |
| Emissions intensity cut (vs 2005) | 45% | 47% |
| Carbon sink | 2.5–3 bn t CO₂e | 3.5–4 bn t CO₂e |
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say the 2022 NDC emission intensity target was 44% (by 2030)
- ⚠ The notes give the 2030 intensity target as 45% in one line and 44% in the 2022 NDC line
- Extra carbon sink: 3.5-4.0 billion t CO2e by 2035; Non-fossil power capacity: the 50% non-fossil target achieved in 2025
- Non-fossil share already ~52.6% (early 2026); ⚠ India uses emissions intensity, not absolute emissions
## 2. Emissions data (CREA / Carbon Brief)
- CO₂ emissions rose only 0.7% in 2025 — slowest since 2001 (excluding 2020)
- 2025 additions: 47 GW solar, 6.3 GW wind, 4 GW hydro, 0.6 GW nuclear
- Plans: 100 GW new coal in 7 years; $1 trillion petrochemicals by 2040; CEA — 786 GW non-fossil by 2035-36 (~70%)
- Forest cover ~24% vs 33% goal
## 3. Background
- UNFCCC: adopted 1992 (Rio), in force 1994; CBDR; Annex I / Non-Annex I
- Paris Agreement: COP21, 2015; in force 2016; well below 2°C, preferably 1.5°C; NDCs updated every 5 years
- UNEP Emissions Gap Report 2025 titled "Off Target"; WRI — NDCs close <14% of gap; COP28 UAE Consensus — triple RE, double efficiency by 2030
## 4. CREA report
- Emissions growth 0.5% in second half of 2025; coal use in thermal plants down 20%; CREA est. 2019, HQ Helsinki
## 5. CEA National Generation Adequacy Plan (2026–27 to 2035–36)
- Part of 20th Electric Power Survey; capacity 520 GW (Jan 2026) → 1,121 GW by 2035–36; peak demand 459 GW; energy need 3,365 BU
| Source (2035–36) | Capacity |
| Nuclear / Gas / Biomass / Small hydro | 22 / 20 / 16 / 6 GW |
- Non-fossil 786 GW — ~49% of generation; CEA est. 1951
## 6. Progress figures
- NDC covers 2031–2035; emission intensity already cut 36% (2005–2020); non-fossil share 52.57% (Feb 2026); carbon sink 2.29 bn t created by 2021

| Degraded land | 29.77% (97 million ha) — Desertification and Land Degradation Atlas |
| Forest carbon stock | 7,285.5 million tonnes |
| Protected areas | Slightly above 5% of area — far below 30×30 |
| Agroforestry | ~8.65% of area |
## 3. Background
- CBD entered into force on 29 December 1993; three objectives — conservation, sustainable use, fair benefit-sharing
- KMGBF: 23 global targets incl. 30×30 (30% land and oceans by 2030)

### World Wildlife Day 2026 — medicinal and aromatic plants
## 1. Day and theme
- Date: 3 March — marks adoption of CITES in 1973
- Theme 2026: "Medicinal and Aromatic Plants: Conserving Health, Heritage and Livelihoods"
- 70–95% of people in developing countries rely on traditional medicine
## 2. India's medicinal plant wealth
- India — one of 17 mega-biodiverse countries; ~7% of global biodiversity; ~45,000 plant species, ~15,000 medicinal, ~8,000 used in AYUSH systems
- ~70% of MAPs in Western Ghats, Eastern Ghats, Himalaya, Aravalli
- BSI documented 5,250 species, 9,500+ traditional claims
- Ved & Goraya (2017): demand ~5,12,000 t/year; 1,178 species traded; 242 in high volume (>100 t)
## 3. Conservation
- In-situ: 115 MPCAs (Medicinal Plants Conservation Areas)
- Ex-situ: 9,361 accessions at NBPGR, New Delhi; seed banks (long-term –10°C to –20°C; medium-term 0–5°C); Svalbard Global Seed Vault (Norway) — largest
- NMPB under Ministry of AYUSH; Central Sector Scheme on medicinal plants — ₹322.41 crore (2021–22 to 2025–26)
- Aushadhi Vanaspati Mitra programme; e-CHARAK app/portal for trade; cultivation now under MIDH

### Kashmir's shrinking snowfall — "death of winter"
## 1. News
- Kashmir: ⚠ seven consecutive winters with below-normal snowfall (every winter since 2019–20)
| Indicator | Value |
| Winter precipitation (Dec–Feb 2025–26) | 100.6 mm vs normal 284.9 mm — 65% deficit |
| February precipitation | 14.2 mm vs 130.4 mm — ~89% deficit |
## 2. Impacts
- Apples, cherries, walnuts lack chilling hours; pests (aphids, mites) proliferate; winter tourism (Gulmarg, Pahalgam) hit
## 3. Western Disturbances
- Originate in the Mediterranean; move east with the subtropical westerly jet; ⚠ bring winter rain/snow to north-west India, not south
### Honey bee attacks and beekeeping
## 1. News
- Bee attack deaths in UP, Gujarat, Maharashtra; ⚠ Uttar Pradesh considering bee attacks as a state-notified disaster
- Main species: Apis dorsata (Giant Rock Bee) — open hives on cliffs/forts; up to 60,000 bees; chases at ~35 km/h; more aggressive in summer
- Bee dies after a single sting; swatting releases pheromones; risk of anaphylaxis
## 2. National Beekeeping and Honey Mission (NBHM)
- Central Sector Scheme — "Sweet Revolution"; outlay ₹500 crore, FY 2020–21 to 2025–26; under Atmanirbhar Bharat
- Implemented by National Bee Board (NBB) (Ministry of Agriculture); 3 Mini Missions
- Madhukranti portal — registration, traceability
| Indicator | Value |
| Exports FY 2023–24 | 1.07 lakh MT — USD 177.55 million |
| Export rank | ⚠ Second-largest (from 9th in 2020) |
- Waggle dance — honeybees; decoded by Karl von Frisch (Nobel)
### Amrabad Tiger Reserve turns Lantana into biocharcoal
## 1. News
- Pilot project in Amrabad Tiger Reserve (ATR) converts invasive Lantana camara into biocharcoal — "black gold"
- Method: uprooted lantana covered with soil and burned in low-oxygen conditions; initial output ~3 kg biochar
- Price: ~₹7,000 per tonne; livelihoods through Joint Forest Management Committees (JFMCs)
## 2. About Lantana camara
- Native to Central and South America; spread globally in the 17th century as an ornamental
- ⚠ IUCN — among the world's worst invasive species
- Spreads by: allelopathy (chemicals suppress nearby plants), bird/monkey seed dispersal, deep roots, resin-coated leaves (drought resistance); toxic to livestock

### Himalayan passes in news — Lipulekh and Zoji La
## 1. Lipulekh Pass
- India–China (Tibet) border trade via Lipulekh (Pithoragarh, Uttarakhand) resumed after six years; season June–September
- Altitude ~5,334 m; route for Kailash Mansarovar Yatra; other trade passes Nathu La (Sikkim), Shipki La (HP)
## 2. Zoji La
- Major avalanche at Zoji La, Kargil (Ladakh) — ~11,650 ft on NH-1 (Srinagar–Kargil–Leh); "Mountain Pass of Blizzards"
- Recaptured by Indian Army 1 November 1948 — Operation Bison

## 2. Panama Canal

- Torrijos–Carter Treaties; handed to Panama 31 December 1999; ~6% of maritime trade; Neo-Panamax locks (2016)

# MCQ NOTES OF APP TOPIC 6:78
### Updated NDC (2035)
## NDC & Paris
- Updated NDC — approved by the Union Cabinet for UNFCCC
- Target 60% non-fossil installed capacity by 2035
- Set in emissions intensity (not absolute); non-fossil share about 52.6% in early 2026
- 50% non-fossil capacity reached in 2025 (five years ahead of 2030)
- Paris Agreement — 2015; does not clearly name fossil fuels
- UNEP Emissions Gap Report 2025 — 'Off Target'
## Water & heat
- India — about 4% of world freshwater, 17% of population
- IMD fixed limits — 45°C heat wave, 47°C severe heat wave
- Jamuguda solar irrigation (Odisha) — 21 families, 35 acres
- Mangroves — store 3–5 times more carbon per hectare than tropical forests
- Kashmir's shrinking snowfall — fruit crops (apples, cherries, walnuts) got inadequate chilling hours
## Lantana & passes
- Lantana camara — native to Central and South America; spreads by allelopathy (chemicals block other plants), animals spread seeds, deep roots, resin-coated leaves; poisons livestock
- Lipulekh Pass — about 5,334 m; Kailash Mansarovar Yatra route
- Zoji La (NH-1) — 'Mountain Pass of Blizzards'
### International Big Cat Alliance
## IBCA
- Launched 9 April 2023; seven big cats (rhinoceros not included); secretariat in India (not Geneva)
- First IBCA Summit — hosted by India, 1–3 June 2026, New Delhi; ~95 countries; outcome 'Delhi Declaration'
- New Delhi Declaration — first declaration only on big cats
- 27th member — Bangladesh (July 2026)
## Big cats
| Animal | Scientific name |
| Asiatic lion | Panthera leo persica |
| Bengal tiger | Panthera tigris tigris |
| Indian leopard | Panthera pardus fusca |
| Snow leopard | Panthera uncia |
| Clouded leopard | Neofelis nebulosa |
- Asiatic lion in the wild — only Gir; India holds about 75% of world's wild tigers
- Tiger — Schedule I, WPA 1972; Project Tiger — 1973
- Cheetah declared extinct in India — 1952
- Project Cheetah third site — Veerangana Durgavati Tiger Reserve (MP); Palamu TR — AI-based human-elephant conflict research centre
## Protected areas & species
- D'Ering Memorial WLS — near Pasighat, East Siang; tiger camera-trapped first time in nearly two decades
- Shekha Jheel — near Aligarh, UP; freshwater wetland
- Discodoris cebuensis (sea slug) — first India record at Haji Ali, Mumbai; Pushkarmema curajae — cyanobacterium, Pushkar Lake, Ajmer
- ZSI — India's first COI-gene DNA barcode library for cockroaches
- ZSI (1916) and BSI (1890) — both HQ Kolkata; Chennai — National Biodiversity Authority
## Climate & others
- Global Stocktake — every five years; no binding targets or fines
- NDCs — updated every five years; Paris adopted COP21 (2015), in force 2016
- Forest cover — about 24%; goal 33%
- Ramsar — India joined 1 February 1982 (convention 1971)
- First conference on transition away from fossil fuels (24–29 April 2026) — Santa Marta, Colombia; 50+ countries
- Kalai-II hydropower (1,200 MW) — Anjaw district; Kamala project — Kamle, Kra Daadi, Kurung Kumey
- Urtan (MP) — first underground coal mine to produce under commercial auctions; JMS Mining
- Lipulekh Pass (Pithoragarh) — India–China trade resumed after six years (March 2026); Nathu La — Sikkim; Shipki La — HP; Zoji La — Kargil
- EP Act, 1986 — under Article 253 (implementing international treaties)

### Shekha Jheel Bird Sanctuary — India's 99th Ramsar site
## 1. Key facts
- Shekha Jheel Bird Sanctuary, near Aligarh (Uttar Pradesh) — designated India's 99th Ramsar site (announced April 22, 2026, by Environment Minister Bhupender Yadav)
- Freshwater wetland; 180+ migratory bird species in winter; on Central Asian Flyway
- India's Ramsar sites: 26 (2014) → 99 (2026) — nearing 100
| Batch | Sites |
| 2026 (to 98) | Patna Bird Sanctuary (UP, as per source), Chhari-Dhand (Gujarat) |
| 2025 | Siliserh Lake (Rajasthan), Kopra Reservoir (Chhattisgarh), Goga Beel, Gokul Reservoir, Udaipur Jheel (Bihar), Menar, Khichan (Rajasthan) |
| World Wetlands Day | Sakkarakottai, Thirthangal (TN), Khecheopalri (Sikkim), Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) |
## 2. Background
- First Indian Ramsar sites: Chilika Lake and Keoladeo NP; most sites — Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh
- Largest — Sundarbans; smallest — Vembannur Wetland Complex (TN; earlier: Renuka, HP)
- Convention signed 1971; India joined February 1, 1982; ~1.4 million ha — first in Asia, third globally
### Tiger "Explorer" from Tadoba returns to East Godavari
## 1. News
- Adult male tiger "Explorer" from Tadoba-Andhari Tiger Reserve (Maharashtra) re-entered East Godavari after crossing the Godavari
- Tranquilised near Kurmapuram village (Feb 6, 2026); released in Papikonda National Park (Feb 14) on NTCA directions; roams Korukonda mandal near Munagala; no human attacks
## 2. Papikonda National Park
- East & West Godavari; ~1,012.86 sq km (CARE; a daily page says 86 sq km); Papikondalu hills, Eastern Ghats; both banks of Godavari
- Reserved Forest 1882 → Wildlife Sanctuary 1978 → National Park 2008; tropical moist deciduous; tiger, gaur, mouse deer
- ⚠ Papikonda is a National Park, not a Tiger Reserve
- Tadoba-Andhari: Chandrapur district; named after Tadoba Lake and Andhari River
## 3. Related facts
| Big cat | Scientific name | IUCN |
| Bengal Tiger | Panthera tigris tigris | Endangered |
| Asiatic Lion | Panthera leo persica | Endangered |
| Indian Leopard | Panthera pardus fusca | Vulnerable |
| Snow Leopard | Panthera uncia | Vulnerable |
| Clouded Leopard | Neofelis nebulosa | Vulnerable |
- Tiger — Schedule I, WPA 1972; Project Tiger 1973; core + buffer zones
- Godavari: rises at Trimbakeshwar; second-longest river of India; Coringa mangroves in delta
- Pulicat Lake — brackish lagoon; Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam TR — AP + Telangana; Sri Venkateswara NP — ⚠ Eastern Ghats (Tirumala hills)
### IBCA "New Delhi Declaration" for big cat summit
## 1. News
- International Big Cat Alliance (IBCA) preparing "New Delhi Declaration" for global big cat summit likely on June 1, 2026 — first international declaration exclusively on big cats
- Focus: habitat connectivity, wildlife crime, finance, science, community participation, One Health
## 2. IBCA facts
- Launched by PM Narendra Modi on 9 April 2023 — 50 years of Project Tiger
- Treaty-based organisation from 23 January 2025; ⚠ HQ/secretariat in India (not Geneva)
- 24 member countries (2026); observers incl. Kazakhstan, Namibia, Thailand
- Seven big cats: tiger, lion, leopard, snow leopard, cheetah, jaguar, puma (⚠ no rhinoceros); India has five
## 3. India's role
- ~75% of world's wild tigers; 50+ Tiger Reserves; NTCA under MoEFCC; Project Tiger 1973
- Asiatic lion only in Gir; snow leopard ~10–15% of global population — ⚠ Vulnerable (downlisted 2017)
- Project Cheetah (2022) — Kuno NP (MP); cheetah declared extinct in India 1952
- IUCN: 1948, Gland, Switzerland — ⚠ not a UN agency; Red List informs CITES; WWF + IUCN created TRAFFIC
### River Basin Management Scheme extended to 2030–31
## 1. News
- RBM Scheme continued for 2026–27 to 2030–31 — outlay ₹2,183 crore (previous cycle ₹1,276 crore); Ministry of Jal Shakti
- Treats whole river system (tributaries, floodplains, groundwater, wetlands) as one hydrological unit
- India: ~4% of world freshwater, ~17% of population
## 2. Features
- Flood/erosion control in NE — e.g. Majuli Island (Assam); springshed management in hills
## 3. Institutions
| Body | Role |
| Central Water Commission (CWC) | Planning, flood forecasting |
| National Water Development Agency (NWDA) | Interlinking of Rivers — e.g. Ken-Betwa Link |
| Brahmaputra Board | Flood, erosion in Brahmaputra and Barak valleys (⚠ not coastal erosion) |
- Brahmaputra: Yarlung Tsangpo in Tibet; Great Bend near Namcha Barwa; enters India as Siang/Dihang (Arunachal); higher discharge/sediment than Indus
- ⚠ Fully centrally funded; basins covered include Brahmaputra, Barak, Teesta, Indus; Indus Waters Treaty 1960; Ministry of Jal Shakti formed 2019
### Great Indian Bustard chick via "jumpstart method" goes missing in Kutch
## 1. News
- GIB chick hatched March 26, 2026 at Naiya grassland, Kutch (Gujarat) — first successful jumpstart method use in Gujarat; flew ~April 18, then missing 4–5 days (likely predation); not geotagged
- Jumpstart: infertile egg of wild female replaced with fertile egg from Rajasthan captive centre; wild mother raises chick
- Gujarat has only three surviving wild female GIBs
## 2. GIB facts
- Ardeotis nigriceps; IUCN Critically Endangered; WPA Schedule I; CITES Appendix I; CMS
- State bird of Rajasthan; arid/semi-arid grasslands; global population < 200
- Biggest threat: power line collisions; lays one egg at a time
- Captive breeding: Ramdevra and Sam (Jaisalmer); Species Recovery Programme under IDWH (100% central assistance), with WII
## 3. Related species status
| Species | IUCN |
| White-bellied Heron | Critically Endangered |
| Himalayan Quail | Critically Endangered |
| Asiatic Lion | Endangered |
- Jerdon's Courser — endemic to Eastern Ghats (AP); rediscovered 1986

### Climate change reshaping disease patterns in India
## 1. Key findings
- ~40% of districts at high risk from extreme weather
- Dengue spreading to non-traditional areas — Shimla, parts of J&K
- Climate change = "health-risk multiplier"
## 2. Policy response
| Initiative | Key facts |
| NPCCHH | National Programme on Climate Change and Human Health — 2018, MoHFW |
| NAPCC | 2008; 8 national missions |
## 3. Related static GK
- Global Stocktake (Paris Agreement) — every five years, reviews collective progress (no targets/penalties)
- 1.5°C measured against pre-industrial (1850–1900) levels
- Blue carbon — mangroves, seagrass, salt marshes; mangroves store 3–5 times more carbon per ha than tropical forests
### Climate adaptation — Tamil Nadu Climate Resilient Villages model
- COP30 — stressed Locally Led Adaptation
### Earthquake Lights (EQL)
- Earthquake Lights — charges from tectonic stress ionise the air; before or during quakes
### Improved biomass cookstoves vs LPG
## 1. Key facts
- Improved Cookstoves (ICS): secondary aeration → fuller combustion
- Thermal efficiency: traditional chulha ~10%; ICS 38–45%; fuel use cut up to two-thirds
- Cost: household ICS < ₹2,000; commercial up to ₹20,000; eligible for carbon credits
- ⚠ ICS reduce but do not eliminate indoor pollution; reduce black carbon
## 2. Pollutant facts
- Photochemical smog: NOx + VOCs in sunlight → ozone, PAN
- Secondary pollutants: ozone, PAN; ⚠ CO is primary
### Rare earth mining pollutes Mekong tributaries
## 1. News
- Unregulated rare earth mining in Myanmar and Laos — heavy metals (arsenic, mercury, lead, cadmium) in tributaries Sai and Ruak → Mekong; threat to "world's kitchen"
## 2. Mekong facts
- Longest river of Southeast Asia; rises in Tibetan Plateau as Lancang; flows through China, Myanmar, Laos, Thailand, Cambodia, Vietnam → South China Sea
- Mekong Delta (Vietnam) — "rice bowl" of SE Asia; Tonle Sap Lake (Cambodia) — seasonal flow reversal
- Mekong River Commission (1995): ⚠ four full members — Cambodia, Laos, Thailand, Vietnam; China, Myanmar = dialogue partners
## 3. Rare earth facts
- 17 elements — lanthanides + scandium + yttrium
- Lanthanum — optical glass; Dysprosium — NdFeB magnets (EV motors)
- Monazite contains thorium, uranium; ionic clay deposits — in-situ leaching; ⚠ froth flotation cannot separate individual REEs
### Yana Caves declared national Geo-heritage site
## 1. Key facts
- Yana Caves near Gokarna, Uttara Kannada (Karnataka) — declared by Geological Survey of India on April 13, 2026 (176th GSI foundation day)
- ~2,700 million years old; Western Ghats; tectonic + karst processes
### Volcano eruptions — Semeru, Sakurajima, Santiaguito
- Mount Semeru (Mahameru) — Indonesia's highest active volcano; highest peak on Java
- Sakurajima — near Kagoshima, Japan; Santiaguito — lava dome, Guatemala (from Santa Maria eruption)
### Sukhna and Kali Tiger Reserve eco-sensitive zones
## 1. Key facts
- Sukhna WLS (Chandigarh): Shivalik foothills; est. 1998; Sukhna Lake created 1958 by Le Corbusier
- Kali Tiger Reserve (Uttara Kannada, Karnataka): 663.32 sq km ESZ proposed; = Dandeli WLS + Anshi NP; declared 2007, renamed 2015 after Kali River; links Bhimgad, Radhanagari, Koyna
- Barasingha WLS (Uttar Pradesh): 408.7 sq km ESZ notified (April 2026)
- ESZs under Environment Protection Act, 1986
### India's first national Bats Report
## 1. Key facts
- First national report on bats (2024–25) — Zoological Survey of India with Nature Conservation Foundation and Bat Conservation International
- India: 135 bat species, 16 endemic, 7 Threatened (IUCN)
- ZSI: 1916, Kolkata; NCF: 1996, Mysuru; BCI: 1982, Austin
### India's updated NDC for 2035
- UNFCCC — adopted 1992 at Rio; in force 1994
### Project Cheetah — nine cheetahs from Botswana
## 1. News
- 9 cheetahs (3 males, 6 females) from Botswana to Kuno NP (Sheopur, MP) via IAF aircraft to Gwalior
- Project Cheetah: Launched 17 Sep 2022, Kuno NP, MP; 9 from Botswana (Mar 2026)
- India now has 48 cheetahs; 39 cubs born in 2023, 27 surviving; some moved to Gandhi Sagar
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say the population was 53 (Mar 2026)
| Phase | Source | Number |
| I (Sept 2022) | Namibia | 8 |
| II (Feb 2023) | South Africa | 12 |
| III (2026) | Botswana | 9 |
- Declared extinct in India 1952; agencies NTCA and WII; African cheetah IUCN Vulnerable
- Third habitat: Veerangana Durgavati Tiger Reserve — merger of Nauradehi and Durgavati sanctuaries; Sagar, Damoh, Narsinghpur (Bundelkhand); soft-release boma
- 3rd site: Veerangana Durgavati TR (MP)
- ⚠ Cheetah sources in order: Namibia → South Africa → Botswana (8 → 12 → 9)
### World Wildlife Day 2026 — medicinal and aromatic plants
## 1. Day and theme
- Date: 3 March — marks adoption of CITES in 1973
- Theme 2026: "Medicinal and Aromatic Plants: Conserving Health, Heritage and Livelihoods"
- 70–95% of people in developing countries rely on traditional medicine
## 2. India's medicinal plant wealth
- India — one of 17 mega-biodiverse countries; ~7% of global biodiversity; ~45,000 plant species, ~15,000 medicinal, ~8,000 used in AYUSH systems
- ~70% of MAPs in Western Ghats, Eastern Ghats, Himalaya, Aravalli
- BSI documented 5,250 species, 9,500+ traditional claims
- Ved & Goraya (2017): demand ~5,12,000 t/year; 1,178 species traded; 242 in high volume (>100 t)
## 3. Conservation
- In-situ: 115 MPCAs (Medicinal Plants Conservation Areas)
- Ex-situ: 9,361 accessions at NBPGR, New Delhi; seed banks (long-term –10°C to –20°C; medium-term 0–5°C); Svalbard Global Seed Vault (Norway) — largest
- NMPB under Ministry of AYUSH; Central Sector Scheme on medicinal plants — ₹322.41 crore (2021–22 to 2025–26)
- Aushadhi Vanaspati Mitra programme; e-CHARAK app/portal for trade; cultivation now under MIDH
## 4. GI-tagged medicinal products
| Product | State |
