### India's wetlands — World Wetlands Day 2026
## 1. Ramsar status
- World Wetlands Day: 2 February 2026 — theme "Wetlands and traditional knowledge: Celebrating cultural heritage"
- Ramsar sites: 26 (2014) → 98 (2026) covering 1,384,140 ha — ⚠ highest in Asia
- Traditional systems: kulam tank cascades (Tamil Nadu); kenis shallow wells, 200+ years old (Wayanad, Kerala); traditional fishing in Srikakulam (AP)
## 2. Examples in news
| Wetland | Fact |
| Kole Wetlands | Kerala — below sea level rice; USD 54 million/year |
| East Kolkata Wetlands | save ₹4,680 million/year treating sewage |
| Bhitarkanika | mangroves reduced damage in Cyclone Dana (2024) |
| Tso Kar | Ladakh — high-altitude Ramsar site |
| Renuka Lake | Himachal — protected by religious norms |
| Harike | migratory birds down 12% (2021–23) |
## 3. Schemes and measures
- MISHTI: restore 540 sq km mangroves; ~4.5 million tonnes sequestration potential
- Amrit Dharohar (2023): livelihood + eco-tourism; first-phase sites Sultanpur, Sirpur, Yashwant Sagar, Bhitarkanika, Chilika; training ALP and Paryatan Navik Certificate
- Wetland City Accreditation: ⚠ Indore and Udaipur — India's first WCA cities (January 2025)
- Mission Sahbhagita / Wetland Mitras: 2 million sensitised; 80,000 wetlands surveyed; 6,200 health cards; 18,000 Wetland Mitras
- Green Credit Programme: credits for mangrove restoration since 2023
- SC ground-truthing: wetlands below 2.25 ha; 1,70,000 ground-truthed, 1,00,000 demarcated; 2017 SC penalty ₹50,000 for no inventories
- Revised NPCA guidelines: catchment-scale management
- Blue carbon: mangroves/peatlands sequester 10–50 times faster than forests
- Losses: India lost nearly one-third of wetlands in three decades; India on Central Asian Flyway
## 4. Background
- ⚠ Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules 2010 cover catchment areas; Ramsar does not make it mandatory to protect all wetlands
- Wetlands International — ⚠ an NGO, not intergovernmental
## 5. New Ramsar sites (total 98)
- Bhupender Yadav announced: Patna Bird Sanctuary (Etah, UP) and Chhari-Dhand (Kutch, Gujarat) → 98 sites (276%+ growth from 26 in 2014)
| Added | Sites |
| 2025 | Siliserh Lake (Alwar, Rajasthan), Kopra Reservoir (Bilaspur, Chhattisgarh) → 96 |
| 2025 (Bihar) | Gogabeel, Gokul Reservoir (Buxar), Udaypur Jheel (West Champaran) |
| World Environment Day | Menar (Udaipur), Khichan (Phalodi) — Rajasthan |
| World Wetlands Day | Sakkarakottai and Therthangal (TN), Khecheopalri (Sikkim), Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) |
- First sites: Chilika and Keoladeo; most sites: Tamil Nadu (20), Uttar Pradesh (11)
- Largest: Sundarbans; smallest: Vembannur Wetland Complex (TN; earlier: Renuka, HP)
- Convention: 1971, Ramsar, Iran; India joined 1 February 1982
- ⚠ India 1st in Asia, 3rd globally; 56 added after 2020; ~1.4 million ha
### NGT upholds Great Nicobar Island Project clearance
## 1. Verdict
- NGT upheld environmental clearance for ₹80,000–81,000 crore Great Nicobar Island Project — "adequate safeguards"
## 2. Project
- Conceived by: NITI Aayog; launched 2021; aligned with Maritime Vision 2030, Amrit Kaal Vision 2047
- Components: International Container Transshipment Terminal (ICTT); greenfield international airport (dual-use); township; gas and solar power plant
- Tribal safeguards: AAJVS, Ministry of Tribal Affairs; Jarawa Policy (2004), Shompen Policy (2015); NCST under Article 338A(9)
- 8 wildlife corridors; compensatory afforestation in Haryana (and MP); island forest cover 75%+
## 3. Concerns
- ~130 sq km rainforest (~15% of island); 10 million+ trees; leatherback turtle nesting at Galathea Bay (sanctuary denotified)
- ~700 ha allegedly in prohibited zones under ICRZ Notification 2019
- Strategic: near Malacca, Sunda, Lombok straits; Sabang port (Indonesia); Kra Canal (Thailand); China's presence at Coco Islands (Myanmar)
## 4. Background
- ⚠ Ten Degree Channel — separates Andaman and Nicobar; Nine Degree — Minicoy from rest of Lakshadweep; Eight Degree — Minicoy and Maldives
- Barren Island — India's only active volcano, ~135 km NE of Port Blair; erupted after 1991 too (2005, 2017, 2018)
## 5. CA360 details
- NGT order dated 16 February 2026 — 6-member bench led by Chairperson Justice Prakash Shrivastava; dismissed fresh petitions
- Noted review of 2022 Environmental Clearance by a High-Powered Committee
- Development area 166 sq km; forest diversion ~130 sq km; tribes Shompen and Nicobarese
### Solid Waste Management Rules, 2026
## 1. Basics
- Notified by: MoEFCC; in force 1 April 2026; ⚠ replaces SWM Rules, 2016
- Waste data: 620 lakh tonnes/year; ~1.85 lakh t/day generated; 1.14 lakh t/day processed; ~40,000 t/day landfilled
- Hierarchy: Prevention → Reduction → Reuse → Recycling → Recovery → Disposal (last resort)
## 2. Four-stream segregation (mandatory)
- ⚠ SWM Rules 2016 had three categories (wet, dry, domestic hazardous)
## 3. Bulk Waste Generators
- Definition: built-up area ≥ 20,000 sq m, or water use ≥ 40,000 litres/day, or waste ≥ 100 kg/day
- Extended Bulk Waste Generator Responsibility (EBWGR): on-site wet waste processing, portal registration, certification-based compliance
- Bulk generators produce ~30% of solid waste
## 4. Enforcement and energy
- Polluter Pays: environmental compensation; higher landfill fees for unsegregated waste; CPCB frames guidelines, SPCBs enforce
- Centralised online portal tracking full lifecycle
- Legacy dumpsites mapped by 31 October 2026
- Waste with calorific value ≥ 1,500 kcal/kg → energy recovery (RDF, co-processing)
- RDF in industries: 6% initially → 15% within six years
- District Collectors oversee landfills; special provisions for hilly areas and islands
## 5. Four-bin colours (CA360)
| Bin | Waste |
| Red | sanitary — wrapped separately |
### Elephant crisis in central India and SAIEE 2021–25
## 1. Conflict
- "Elephant emergency" after attacks in Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha
- <8% of elephants cause ~50% of conflict casualties
- Causes: droughts 1982–83, 1986–87 (El Niño); mining in Singhbhum (Jharkhand), Keonjhar and Sundargarh (Odisha); Rengali Dam (1985); linear infrastructure
- Spread: West Bengal from Dalma (1986) — ~200 in Jangalmahal; Chhattisgarh — Surguja, Surajpur, Hasdeo Arand; MP — Bandhavgarh, Sanjay-Dubri; Maharashtra — Gadchiroli, Tadoba-Andhari
## 2. SAIEE 2021–25 census
- By: Wildlife Institute of India (WII) under Project Elephant (1992) — "Status of Elephants in India: DNA-based Synchronous All-India Population Estimation"
- Population: 22,446 vs 29,964 (2017) — decline ~25%
- Declines in Western Ghats and NE Hills & Brahmaputra Plains; Shivalik–Gangetic nearly unchanged
## 3. Background
- Herds are matriarchal; gestation 18–22 months (longest among mammals)
### Himalaya–Karakoram GLOF risks poorly assessed
## 1. Study
- Journal: npj Natural Hazards (January 2026) — GLOF risks in the Himalaya–Karakoram poorly assessed; ~1 million people downstream at risk
- GLOF: natural dam holding a glacial lake collapses — sudden surge with debris
## 2. Numbers — 388 GLOF events
| Country | Events |
| Pakistan | 131 |
| India | 59 |
| Nepal | 54 |
- Since 1990: number of glacial lakes +53%, area +51%, volume +48%
- Driver: elevation-dependent warming (higher altitudes warm faster); lake-terminating glaciers retreat faster
## 3. India's GLOFs
- Sikkim 2023: lake wall collapse — 55 deaths
- ⚠ Ghepang Ghat Glacial Lake — Lahaul and Spiti, Himachal Pradesh (~4,068 m) — grew 178% (36.49 ha in 1989 to 101.30 ha in 2022) per ISRO
### NDMA guidelines for Disaster Victim Identification
## 1. Guidelines
- Issued by: NDMA — ⚠ India's first-ever national guidelines and SOPs for Disaster Victim Identification (DVI)
- Triggers: Air India crash (Ahmedabad), Sangareddy chemical explosion, Uttarakhand flash floods, Delhi car bomb blast
- Four stages: systematic recovery → post-mortem data → ante-mortem data → reconciliation
- National Dental Data Registry — teeth/jaws survive extreme conditions; forensic odontology and archaeology
- ⚠ Mass autopsies discouraged (not mandated)
- "Humanitarian forensics"; forensic teams in every state; INTERPOL best practices

## 2. Goa Bird Atlas

### Cyclone Gezani hits Madagascar
## 1. Cyclone
- Struck Madagascar (Feb 2026) — landfall on east coast, port city Toamasina; at least 36 deaths
### Paris Agreement — 10 years
## 1. News
- Completed 10 years in November 2025; re-evaluation after US exit (early 2025) and COP30 (Belém, Brazil)
## 2. Background
- Adopted: 2015, COP21 Paris, under UNFCCC; legally binding framework, voluntary national targets
- NDCs every 5 years; Global Stocktake every 5 years — first at COP28 (2023)
- Finance: USD 100 billion/yr till 2025; NCQG (COP29, 2024) — at least USD 300 billion/yr by 2035; Global South demands USD 1.3 trillion (≥ USD 600 billion grants)
- ⚠ Kyoto Protocol applied only to developed nations (top-down); Paris is bottom-up
## 3. India
- Targets: 45% cut in emissions intensity (from 2005) by 2030; ~50% non-fossil capacity by 2030; carbon sink 2.5–3 billion tonnes CO₂; Net Zero 2070
- ⚠ 50% non-fossil electricity capacity achieved in 2025 — 5 years early
- China: peak before 2030, net zero 2060
### Aravalli range — SC on illegal mining; restoration pilot
## 1. Supreme Court
- Bench: CJI Surya Kant, Joymalya Bagchi, Vipul M. Pancholi — illegal mining severely damaging Aravallis
- Expert committee to be constituted (names in 4 weeks by ASG Aishwarya Bhati and amicus K. Parameshwar)
- Extended stay on its 20 November order on the new definition of Aravalli range
- Protection part of Article 21 jurisprudence
## 2. Restoration pilot
- Sankal Foundation study released by Bhupender Yadav; pilot in four villages around Gurugram
- ~43% households depend on Aravalli forests for fodder, fuelwood, medicinal plants

### Aralam — Kerala's first butterfly sanctuary
## 1. News
- Aralam Wildlife Sanctuary renamed Aralam Butterfly Sanctuary — ⚠ first in Kerala
- Notified: SRO No. 1407/2025 under Section 18(1), WPA 1972; amends 1984 notification
## 2. Facts
- Kannur district; ~55 sq km; river Cheenkanni; Schedule I Slender Loris
- Bordering: Brahmagiri WLS (Karnataka), Kottiyoor WLS
- 266 of Kerala's 327 butterfly species; 8 lakh+ Albatross butterflies on 11 January 2025

### Bomb cyclone hits northern US
## 1. News
- Date: 29 December 2025 — northern United States (Midwest, East Coast)
## 2. Concept
- Bomb cyclone: intense mid-latitude (extra-tropical) storm
- Definition: central pressure drops by at least 24 millibars in 24 hours — process called bombogenesis
- Formation: cold Arctic/Canadian air meets warm moist air (often over oceans)
- Season: any season; most frequent in fall and winter
- Where: mostly western North Atlantic (energy from Gulf Stream); also NW Pacific, occasionally Mediterranean
- Study: Northern Illinois University (2021) — ~7% of non-tropical lows near North America (1979–2019) were bomb cyclones; avg 18 per year
- Hazards: winds 80 kmph+, blizzards, lakeshore flooding, thundersnow (snow with lightning)
| Aspect | Bomb cyclone | Hurricane |
| Type | Mid-latitude (extra-tropical) | Tropical cyclone |
| Energy | Temperature contrast | Warm ocean waters |
| Season | Fall and winter | Summer, early autumn |
| Core | Cold-core | Warm-core |
- ⚠ Bomb cyclones are not powered by warm ocean water (that is hurricanes)
- ⚠ Eye of a tropical cyclone is warmer, not colder, than surroundings
### Urban heat islands and bird deaths
## 1. News
- Urban birds early victims of Urban Heat Island (UHI) — dehydration, collapse during heatwaves
- UHI intensity in India: 2–10°C; surface hotspots 6–8°C hotter
- Ahmedabad (May 2025): Jivdaya Charitable Trust treated ~3,800 birds
- Studies: Bengaluru (2025), Western Ghats (2022), China multi-city (2023)
- Tree-rich areas 1–3°C cooler — e.g. Delhi Ridge, Lodhi Gardens
## 2. Links
- National Mission on Sustainable Habitat; SDG 11 (Sustainable Cities), SDG 15 (Life on Land)

### Aravalli range — SC uniform 100-m definition and mining pause
## 1. News
- Supreme Court (November 2025): adopted a uniform definition — only hills above 100 m elevation are Aravallis; paused all fresh mining leases in Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat
- Direction: prepare a Management Plan for Sustainable Mining (MPSM) for the entire range
- ⚠ No total ban — total bans fuel illegal mining
## 2. Aravalli facts
- Age: nearly two billion years — India's oldest range (⚠ an old UPSC 2001 key gives 370 million years)
- Length: 650 km, Delhi to Gujarat
- Highest peak: Guru Shikhar (1,722 m), Mount Abu, Rajasthan — ⚠ not Gujarat
## 3. Protection timeline

| 2024 (May) | Halt on new mining leases |
| 2024 | Central Empowered Committee (CEC) recommendations |
| 2025 | Aravalli Green Wall Project — 5-km green buffer in 29 districts of four States |
| 2025 (Nov) | Uniform 100-m definition |
- Land restoration goal: 26 million hectares by 2030 (UNCCD)
### Five Tamil Nadu products get GI tags
## 1. News
- Five TN products got GI tags; TN's GI count rises to 74
| Product | Place | Key point |

### Methane emissions from landfills — satellite evidence
## 1. News
- Satellite data: methane from Indian landfills far higher than official estimates; globally landfill emissions up to 1.8 times model estimates
- India: third-largest methane emitter (after China, USA); 31 million tonnes/year (about 9% of global)
- Waste sector: about 15% of India's methane
- 3rd Biennial Update Report (2016): methane 409 million tonnes CO₂e (excl. LULUCF)
## 2. Methane facts
- CH₄: simplest alkane; 80–95% of natural gas; lighter than air
- GWP: 84× CO₂ over 20 years; 28–34× over 100 years
- Platforms: ISRO (2023 study), CarbonMapper – Tanager, SRON, ClimateTRACE, WasteMap
## 3. Landfill hotspots
| City | Landfill |
| Ahmedabad | Pirana |
| Mumbai | Deonar, Kanjurmarg (10× model estimates) |
| Delhi | Ghazipur, Bhalswa |
| Hyderabad | Jawaharnagar |
| Kolkata | Dhapa |
- Example of capture: Indore's Bio-CNG plant under GOBARdhan
### Indoor air pollution and air-purifying plants
## 1. News
- Finding: indoor air can be 2.5 times more polluted than outdoor, up to 100 times in extreme cases
- Burden: indoor pollution linked to 4.1% of global deaths annually
- ⚠ India has no uniform Indoor Air Quality (IAQ) protocol
## 2. WHO data — household air pollution
- Deaths: 3.2 million per year; 86 million healthy life years lost (2019 DALYs)
| Cause of HAP deaths | Share |
| Ischaemic heart disease | 32% |
| Stroke | 23% |
| Lower respiratory infections | 21% |
| COPD | 19% |
- COPD: progressive, irreversible; leading cause tobacco smoking; confirmed by spirometry
## 3. Air-purifying plants
| Plant | Scientific name |
| Areca Palm | Dypsis lutescens |
| Weeping Fig | Ficus benjamina |
| Spider Plant | Chlorophytum comosum |
| Peace Lily | Spathiphyllum wallisii |
| Bamboo Palm | Chamaedorea seifrizii |
- Clean cooking: Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY) for LPG; traditional stoves emit black carbon and methane (SLCPs)
### Bioremediation — why India needs it
## 1. Concept
- Bioremediation: use of bacteria, fungi, algae, plants to break down pollutants into non-toxic by-products (water, CO₂, organic acids)
| Type | Meaning | Example |
| In situ | Clean on the spot | Oil-eating bacteria sprayed on ocean spill |
| Ex situ | Remove, treat in facility, return | Soil washing for pesticide farmland |
- ⚠ Heavy metals (cadmium, lead) cannot be completely degraded — only transformed/immobilised (UPSC 2017)
- GM microbes can be engineered for bioremediation
## 2. India's efforts
- Gaps: no unified national bioremediation standards; no biosafety rules for GM microbes
- Abroad: China uses GM bacteria under its soil pollution law; Japan integrates into city waste systems

### Climate & hazards
## Bomb cyclones
- Bomb cyclone — extra-tropical, mid-latitude, cold-core; energy from temperature contrast
- Forms when cold Arctic/Canadian air meets warm, moist air
- Defining pressure fall — bombogenesis; most frequent in fall and winter
- Warm ocean water powers hurricanes (tropical, warm-core), not bomb cyclones
## Paris Agreement and India
- NDCs submitted every 5 years
- Paris Agreement — bottom-up approach
- India — 45% cut in emissions intensity of GDP from 2005 levels by 2030; Net Zero by 2070
## Himalaya
- GLOF risk study (Jan 2026) — published in npj Natural Hazards; about 1 million people downstream at risk
- ICIMOD — India needs about USD 102 billion annually for Himalayan adaptation and mitigation; HKH region USD 768.7 billion; India + China USD 605 billion

- India's first-ever national guidelines and SOPs for DVI; discourage mass autopsies

- Dugong (Dugong dugon) — "sea cow", plant-eater living on seagrass; in India in Gulf of Mannar and Palk Bay
- Shiveluch (Russia) — stratovolcano with super-hydrous magma; ash up to about 9,000 m
- Mount Bur Ni Telong — Bener Meriah Regency, Aceh, Sumatra; Indonesia raised the alert level and began evacuations
## Dams and rivers
- Sawalkot project — on the Chenab, Ramban district, J&K (linked to India's exit from Indus Waters Treaty)
- Shahpur Kandi — Ravi, Pathankot; national project 2008; costs 80% Punjab, 20% Centre
- Bargi Dam — on the Narmada; first completed dam on the Narmada; National Dam Safety Authority issued show-cause notice to the Narmada Valley Development Authority
- Tehri Dam (260 m) — tallest dam in India
## Hazards
- GLOF — sudden rush of water and debris after the natural dam holding a glacial lake breaks
- Elephant conflict causes — droughts, mining, Rengali Dam (1985), linear infrastructure
### India's wetlands — World Wetlands Day 2026
## 1. Ramsar status
- World Wetlands Day: 2 February 2026 — theme "Wetlands and traditional knowledge: Celebrating cultural heritage"
- Ramsar sites: 26 (2014) → 98 (2026) covering 1,384,140 ha — ⚠ highest in Asia
- Traditional systems: kulam tank cascades (Tamil Nadu); kenis shallow wells, 200+ years old (Wayanad, Kerala); traditional fishing in Srikakulam (AP)
## 2. Examples in news
| Wetland | Fact |
| Kole Wetlands | Kerala — below sea level rice; USD 54 million/year |
| East Kolkata Wetlands | save ₹4,680 million/year treating sewage |
| Bhitarkanika | mangroves reduced damage in Cyclone Dana (2024) |
| Tso Kar | Ladakh — high-altitude Ramsar site |
| Renuka Lake | Himachal — protected by religious norms |
| Harike | migratory birds down 12% (2021–23) |
## 3. Schemes and measures
- MISHTI: restore 540 sq km mangroves; ~4.5 million tonnes sequestration potential
- Amrit Dharohar (2023): livelihood + eco-tourism; first-phase sites Sultanpur, Sirpur, Yashwant Sagar, Bhitarkanika, Chilika; training ALP and Paryatan Navik Certificate
- Wetland City Accreditation: ⚠ Indore and Udaipur — India's first WCA cities (January 2025)
- Mission Sahbhagita / Wetland Mitras: 2 million sensitised; 80,000 wetlands surveyed; 6,200 health cards; 18,000 Wetland Mitras
- Green Credit Programme: credits for mangrove restoration since 2023
- SC ground-truthing: wetlands below 2.25 ha; 1,70,000 ground-truthed, 1,00,000 demarcated; 2017 SC penalty ₹50,000 for no inventories
- Revised NPCA guidelines: catchment-scale management
- Blue carbon: mangroves/peatlands sequester 10–50 times faster than forests
- Losses: India lost nearly one-third of wetlands in three decades; India on Central Asian Flyway
## 4. Background
- ⚠ Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules 2010 cover catchment areas; Ramsar does not make it mandatory to protect all wetlands
- Wetlands International — ⚠ an NGO, not intergovernmental
## 5. New Ramsar sites (total 98)
- Bhupender Yadav announced: Patna Bird Sanctuary (Etah, UP) and Chhari-Dhand (Kutch, Gujarat) → 98 sites (276%+ growth from 26 in 2014)
| Added | Sites |
| 2025 | Siliserh Lake (Alwar, Rajasthan), Kopra Reservoir (Bilaspur, Chhattisgarh) → 96 |
| 2025 (Bihar) | Gogabeel, Gokul Reservoir (Buxar), Udaypur Jheel (West Champaran) |
| World Environment Day | Menar (Udaipur), Khichan (Phalodi) — Rajasthan |
| World Wetlands Day | Sakkarakottai and Therthangal (TN), Khecheopalri (Sikkim), Udhwa Lake (Jharkhand) |
- First sites: Chilika and Keoladeo; most sites: Tamil Nadu (20), Uttar Pradesh (11)
- Largest: Sundarbans; smallest: Vembannur Wetland Complex (TN; earlier: Renuka, HP)
- Convention: 1971, Ramsar, Iran; India joined 1 February 1982
- ⚠ India 1st in Asia, 3rd globally; 56 added after 2020; ~1.4 million ha
### NGT upholds Great Nicobar Island Project clearance
## 1. Verdict
- NGT upheld environmental clearance for ₹80,000–81,000 crore Great Nicobar Island Project — "adequate safeguards"
## 2. Project
- Conceived by: NITI Aayog; launched 2021; aligned with Maritime Vision 2030, Amrit Kaal Vision 2047
- Components: International Container Transshipment Terminal (ICTT); greenfield international airport (dual-use); township; gas and solar power plant
- Tribal safeguards: AAJVS, Ministry of Tribal Affairs; Jarawa Policy (2004), Shompen Policy (2015); NCST under Article 338A(9)
- 8 wildlife corridors; compensatory afforestation in Haryana (and MP); island forest cover 75%+
## 3. Concerns
- ~130 sq km rainforest (~15% of island); 10 million+ trees; leatherback turtle nesting at Galathea Bay (sanctuary denotified)
- ~700 ha allegedly in prohibited zones under ICRZ Notification 2019
- Strategic: near Malacca, Sunda, Lombok straits; Sabang port (Indonesia); Kra Canal (Thailand); China's presence at Coco Islands (Myanmar)
## 4. Background
- ⚠ Ten Degree Channel — separates Andaman and Nicobar; Nine Degree — Minicoy from rest of Lakshadweep; Eight Degree — Minicoy and Maldives
- Barren Island — India's only active volcano, ~135 km NE of Port Blair; erupted after 1991 too (2005, 2017, 2018)
## 5. CA360 details
- NGT order dated 16 February 2026 — 6-member bench led by Chairperson Justice Prakash Shrivastava; dismissed fresh petitions
- Noted review of 2022 Environmental Clearance by a High-Powered Committee
- Development area 166 sq km; forest diversion ~130 sq km; tribes Shompen and Nicobarese
### Solid Waste Management Rules, 2026
## 1. Basics
- Notified by: MoEFCC; in force 1 April 2026; ⚠ replaces SWM Rules, 2016
- Waste data: 620 lakh tonnes/year; ~1.85 lakh t/day generated; 1.14 lakh t/day processed; ~40,000 t/day landfilled
- Hierarchy: Prevention → Reduction → Reuse → Recycling → Recovery → Disposal (last resort)
## 2. Four-stream segregation (mandatory)
- ⚠ SWM Rules 2016 had three categories (wet, dry, domestic hazardous)
## 3. Bulk Waste Generators
- Definition: built-up area ≥ 20,000 sq m, or water use ≥ 40,000 litres/day, or waste ≥ 100 kg/day
- Extended Bulk Waste Generator Responsibility (EBWGR): on-site wet waste processing, portal registration, certification-based compliance
- Bulk generators produce ~30% of solid waste
## 4. Enforcement and energy
- Polluter Pays: environmental compensation; higher landfill fees for unsegregated waste; CPCB frames guidelines, SPCBs enforce
- Centralised online portal tracking full lifecycle
- Legacy dumpsites mapped by 31 October 2026
- Waste with calorific value ≥ 1,500 kcal/kg → energy recovery (RDF, co-processing)
- RDF in industries: 6% initially → 15% within six years
- District Collectors oversee landfills; special provisions for hilly areas and islands
## 5. Four-bin colours (CA360)
| Bin | Waste |
| Red | sanitary — wrapped separately |
### Elephant crisis in central India and SAIEE 2021–25
## 1. Conflict
- "Elephant emergency" after attacks in Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha
- <8% of elephants cause ~50% of conflict casualties
- Causes: droughts 1982–83, 1986–87 (El Niño); mining in Singhbhum (Jharkhand), Keonjhar and Sundargarh (Odisha); Rengali Dam (1985); linear infrastructure
- Spread: West Bengal from Dalma (1986) — ~200 in Jangalmahal; Chhattisgarh — Surguja, Surajpur, Hasdeo Arand; MP — Bandhavgarh, Sanjay-Dubri; Maharashtra — Gadchiroli, Tadoba-Andhari
## 2. SAIEE 2021–25 census
- By: Wildlife Institute of India (WII) under Project Elephant (1992) — "Status of Elephants in India: DNA-based Synchronous All-India Population Estimation"
- Population: 22,446 vs 29,964 (2017) — decline ~25%
- Declines in Western Ghats and NE Hills & Brahmaputra Plains; Shivalik–Gangetic nearly unchanged
## 3. Background
- Herds are matriarchal; gestation 18–22 months (longest among mammals)
### Himalaya–Karakoram GLOF risks poorly assessed
- Elevation-dependent warming — cause of GLOF risk; lake-terminating glaciers retreat faster
- Ghepang Ghat Glacial Lake — Lahaul and Spiti, Himachal Pradesh, about 4,068 m; grew 178% (1989–2022), 36.49 ha → 101.30 ha (ISRO)
### NDMA guidelines for Disaster Victim Identification
## 1. Guidelines
- Issued by: NDMA — ⚠ India's first-ever national guidelines and SOPs for Disaster Victim Identification (DVI)
- Triggers: Air India crash (Ahmedabad), Sangareddy chemical explosion, Uttarakhand flash floods, Delhi car bomb blast
- Four stages: systematic recovery → post-mortem data → ante-mortem data → reconciliation
- National Dental Data Registry — teeth/jaws survive extreme conditions; forensic odontology and archaeology
- ⚠ Mass autopsies discouraged (not mandated)
- "Humanitarian forensics"; forensic teams in every state; INTERPOL best practices

### Bomb cyclone hits northern US
## 1. News
- Date: 29 December 2025 — northern United States (Midwest, East Coast)
## 2. Concept
- Bomb cyclone: intense mid-latitude (extra-tropical) storm
- Definition: central pressure drops by at least 24 millibars in 24 hours — process called bombogenesis
- Formation: cold Arctic/Canadian air meets warm moist air (often over oceans)
- Season: any season; most frequent in fall and winter
- Where: mostly western North Atlantic (energy from Gulf Stream); also NW Pacific, occasionally Mediterranean
- Study: Northern Illinois University (2021) — ~7% of non-tropical lows near North America (1979–2019) were bomb cyclones; avg 18 per year
- Hazards: winds 80 kmph+, blizzards, lakeshore flooding, thundersnow (snow with lightning)
| Aspect | Bomb cyclone | Hurricane |
| Type | Mid-latitude (extra-tropical) | Tropical cyclone |
| Energy | Temperature contrast | Warm ocean waters |
| Season | Fall and winter | Summer, early autumn |
| Core | Cold-core | Warm-core |
- ⚠ Bomb cyclones are not powered by warm ocean water (that is hurricanes)
- ⚠ Eye of a tropical cyclone is warmer, not colder, than surroundings
### Mount Bur Ni Telong volcano alert — Indonesia

- Indonesia raised alert level after surge in seismic activity; evacuations begun
- Location: Bener Meriah Regency, Aceh Province, Sumatra

### Aravalli range — SC uniform 100-m definition and mining pause
## 1. News
- Supreme Court (November 2025): adopted a uniform definition — only hills above 100 m elevation are Aravallis; paused all fresh mining leases in Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat
- Direction: prepare a Management Plan for Sustainable Mining (MPSM) for the entire range
- ⚠ No total ban — total bans fuel illegal mining
## 2. Aravalli facts
- Age: nearly two billion years — India's oldest range (⚠ an old UPSC 2001 key gives 370 million years)
- Length: 650 km, Delhi to Gujarat
- Highest peak: Guru Shikhar (1,722 m), Mount Abu, Rajasthan — ⚠ not Gujarat
## 3. Protection timeline

| 2009 | SC ban on mining in Gurugram, Faridabad, Mewat |
| 2024 (May) | Halt on new mining leases |
| 2024 | Central Empowered Committee (CEC) recommendations |
| 2025 | Aravalli Green Wall Project — 5-km green buffer in 29 districts of four States |
| 2025 (Nov) | Uniform 100-m definition |
- Land restoration goal: 26 million hectares by 2030 (UNCCD)

### Methane emissions from landfills — satellite evidence
## 1. News
- Satellite data: methane from Indian landfills far higher than official estimates; globally landfill emissions up to 1.8 times model estimates
- India: third-largest methane emitter (after China, USA); 31 million tonnes/year (about 9% of global)
- Waste sector: about 15% of India's methane
- 3rd Biennial Update Report (2016): methane 409 million tonnes CO₂e (excl. LULUCF)
## 2. Methane facts
- CH₄: simplest alkane; 80–95% of natural gas; lighter than air
- GWP: 84× CO₂ over 20 years; 28–34× over 100 years
- Platforms: ISRO (2023 study), CarbonMapper – Tanager, SRON, ClimateTRACE, WasteMap
## 3. Landfill hotspots

### Hayli Gubbi volcano erupts after ~12,000 years
## 1. News
- Volcano: Hayli Gubbi, north-eastern Ethiopia (near Red Sea, Afar Rift)
- Erupted: 23 November 2025 — ⚠ first in nearly 12,000 years
- Type: explosive — ash and gases, no lava flows
- Ash plume: via Arabian Peninsula, Arabian Sea → Gujarat, Rajasthan → Delhi, UP → towards China; at 10–14 km altitude — flights disrupted
## 2. Background
| Type | Example |
| Active | Kilauea (Hawaii), Santa Maria (Guatemala) |
| Dormant | Mount Kilimanjaro (Tanzania) |
| Extinct | Dhinodhar Hill (Gujarat) |
| Shield (basaltic, gentle) | Mauna Loa |
| Composite / Stratovolcano (explosive) | Mayon, Mount Fuji, Mount Rainier |
- Mid-ocean ridge system: over 65,000 km

- Study (Marine Pollution Bulletin): arsenic, cadmium, chromium, mercury, lead in 46 stranded dugongs

- Dugong dugon — "sea cow"; ⚠ only herbivorous marine mammal in India; eats seagrass (30–40 kg/day)
- Size: ~3 m, ~420 kg; lives up to 70 years; matures at 9–10 years; calves every 3–5 years

### Dugong conservation — Palk Bay Dugong Conservation Reserve
## 1. Reserve
- Established: 2022 in Palk Bay (Tamil Nadu) under Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972
- Area: over 12,000 hectares of seagrass meadows
- ⚠ First dedicated dugong conservation area in India
- Recognition: hailed by IUCN as an exemplar for marine conservation
## 2. Background
- Dugong (Dugong dugon): 'sea cow', herbivorous marine mammal; found in Gulf of Mannar, Palk Bay, Gulf of Kutch, Andaman & Nicobar
- ⚠ Schedule I of WPA (not Schedule II); ⚠ not found along the entire Indian coast
- Gujarat and Andaman populations are smaller and less protected
### Monsoon 2025 — 8% above normal, floods
## 1. Data
- Overall: 8% above normal (LPA 87 cm)
| Region | Excess |
| Northwest India | 27% (highest) |
| Central India | 15% |
| South Peninsula | 10% |
- Kharif sown area: up 15 lakh ha to 1,110 lakh ha; rice up 8.45 lakh ha to 438 lakh ha
- Reservoir storage: 163 BCM (from 157.8 BCM)
- Floods/landslides: Himachal Pradesh, J&K, Punjab
## 2. Background
- ⚠ Cloudburst (IMD): ≥100 mm rainfall in one hour over a small area (~20–30 sq km) — not 50 mm
- Initiatives: National Monsoon Mission; Flash Flood Guidance Services for 30,000+ watersheds; NDMP 2019; DRIP (dam safety)
### E-waste and public health in India
## 1. Burden
- 2025: India generated 2.2 million MT — ⚠ third-largest after China and USA
- Growth: 150% rise from 0.71 million MT (2017–18); projected to double by 2030
- 65 cities produce >60%; hotspots Seelampur, Mustafabad (Delhi), Moradabad (UP), Bhiwandi (Maharashtra)
- Formal units: 322 registered recyclers (capacity 2.2 MT); only 43% processed formally (2023–24)
- PM2.5 in Seelampur > 300 μg/m³ — 12× WHO 24-hour limit (25 μg/m³)
## 2. Background
- E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022: Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR); mandatory registration of recyclers/dismantlers
- WHO: 18 million children and 13 million women in informal waste zones globally
- Released toxins: beryllium, cadmium, chromium, mercury, lead (UPSC 2013 PYQ)
### Forest Rights Act — 15% claims pending; Centre defends FRA in SC
## 1. Status
| Item | Number |
| Claims received | 51.23 lakh |
| Titles distributed | 25.11 lakh (49.02%) |
| Pending | 7.49 lakh (~15%) |
| Rejected | 18.62 lakh (36.35%) |
## 2. High-pendency states (31 May 2025)
| State | Pending |
| Telangana | 3.29 lakh (~50%) — highest number |
| Odisha | 1.20 lakh |
| Assam | 96,000 (~62%) |
- ⚠ Highest proportional pendency: Goa (87%), Himachal Pradesh (84.5%)
- FRA process not begun in Dima Hasao and Karbi Anglong (Assam)
## 3. Schemes
- Dharti Aaba Janjatiya Gram Utkarsh Abhiyan (DA-JGUA): launched October 2024, named after Birsa Munda; saturation via 17 line ministries; ₹15,000/hectare to Gram Sabhas for CFRMPs; target 1,000 CFRMPs in two years; funds approved for all FRA states except West Bengal; FRA cells functional in Karnataka, TN, Andhra Pradesh, Odisha
- FRA Atlas published by MP, Tamil Nadu, Chhattisgarh
- Adi Karmayogi Abhiyan (September 2025): tribal grassroots leadership in 1 lakh villages
## 4. Centre defends FRA in Supreme Court
- MoTA filed counter affidavit defending FRA 2006 and its 2012 Rules against a petition alleging conflict with wildlife laws
## 5. Background
- FRA, 2006: Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act; nodal — Ministry of Tribal Affairs; implemented by states; Community Forest Resource Rights (CFRR) empower Gram Sabhas
### Global Pledge for Healthy Indoor Air
## 1. News

- Idea: clean indoor air as universal health protection, like clean drinking water

### Global plastic pollution crisis
## 1. Data
- World Environment Day 2025 (5 June) theme: "Ending Plastic Pollution"
- OECD Global Plastic Outlook: production doubled 2000–2019 to 460 million t; waste 353 million t
- Waste fate: recycled 9%, incinerated 19%, landfilled 50%, mismanaged 22%
- Waste by source: packaging 40%, consumer goods 12%, textiles 11%
- 2024: ~500 million t produced, 400 million t waste; 2060 → 1.2 billion t
- Ocean: 11 million t a year enter oceans (Ocean Conservancy); ~200 million t already there
- GHG: plastics 3.4% of emissions; could be 19% of carbon budget by 2040 (UNEP)
- Health letter: 18 million health professionals, 88 countries
## 2. Global response
- UNEA-5 (2022): all 193 members agreed to negotiate a legally binding plastics treaty — ⚠ not a ban on production
- UNEP aim: cut plastic waste 80% in two decades
- EPR: Extended Producer Responsibility
### Minicoy (Lakshadweep) waste fire — 4,000 tonnes
## 1. News

### Emissions may cut surface solar radiation over India by up to 10%
## 1. News
- Study: Ashwin Jadhav and Rohini Bhawar, Savitribai Phule Pune University — journal Energy and Climate Change
- Finding: up to 10% fall in surface solar radiation (SSR) over central, southern, eastern India under intermediate/high emissions — due to aerosols, turbidity, post-monsoon clouds
- Mean SSR loss: ~1.10 W/m² per decade (intermediate), ~1.32 W/m² per decade (high)
- ⚠ Indo-Gangetic Plains and NE may see marginal increase (low emission); north-west India most stable
- India solar capacity: 123,130.13 MW (31 Aug 2025); renewables 37% of capacity but ~18% of generation
### Isobutanol–diesel blending pilot by ARAI
## 1. News
- Announced by: Nitin Gadkari — ARAI (Automotive Research Association of India) studying isobutanol–diesel blending; pilot ~18 months
- Why not ethanol: low flash point, volatility, poor miscibility with diesel
- Isobutanol: ⚠ higher flash point, better miscibility; ⚠ lower cetane number (needs additives); up to 10% blending safe
- Feedstock: sugarcane syrup, B-heavy molasses, grains; a 150 klpd plant → ~125 klpd ethanol + 20 klpd isobutanol
### Environment Audit Rules, 2025
## 1. News
- Environment Audit Rules, 2025 notified to strengthen environmental compliance (headline only in source)

## 1. News

- Drilling: ~1,289 feet below sea floor; ~50,000 litres extracted; cost ~$25 million
- Could supply: New York City for 800 years
## 2. Background
- Offshore aquifers: extend up to 90 km offshore; ~1 million km³ freshwater (~10% of terrestrial freshwater) — 2021 study in Environmental Research Letters
- Origin theories: Ice Age recharge; connection to onshore aquifers; held by clay cap rock
- ⚠ Not recharged by current rainfall directly
- UN 2023: freshwater demand to exceed supply by 40% by 2030

- Disasters cited: flash flood at Dharali (Uttarkashi), landslides in Mandi, floods in J&K

### India's urban future — climate-resilient cities
## 1. Facts
- Jobs: cities to generate over 70% of new jobs in next 5 years
- Urban population: near 1 billion by 2050
- Housing: 144 million new homes needed by 2070; more than half of 2070 housing stock yet to be built
- Flood losses: $5 billion by 2030 → $30 billion by 2070; over 25% urban roads exposed to flooding
- Urban Heat Island: night temperatures 3–5°C higher
- Investment needed: ~$10.95 trillion over 30 years
- Examples: Kolkata flood forecasting; Chennai stormwater; Ahmedabad Heat Action Plan

- FAO: female-headed households lose 3% more income to floods (~$35/person/yr)

### Monsoon 2025 disasters and need for preventive resilience
## 1. News
- August 2025: floods in Punjab and Delhi; landslides in Himachal Pradesh; deaths in J&K

### 33 years after the Rio Earth Summit
## 1. Background
- Summit: UNCED, 1992, Rio — 125 countries, 100 heads of state, 12,000 delegates
- Outcomes: UNFCCC (in force 1994); CBDR principle in Rio Declaration; Biodiversity Convention; Desertification Treaty
- ⚠ Binding Forest Convention was not adopted — blocked by Global South (G77, India); only non-binding forest principles
- Paris Agreement (2015): shift to voluntary NDCs
- Climate finance promise: $100 billion a year — unmet
### Oil and gas well blowouts — ONGC Sivasagar
## 1. News
- Event: blowout at an ONGC well in Sivasagar district, Assam on 12 June 2025
- Evacuated: over 1,500 residents
- Experts: well-control experts from the United States
- Controlled: by 21 June
## 2. Background
- Blowout: uncontrolled release of gas/oil when well pressure beats blowout preventers (BOPs) and drilling mud
- Causes: faulty valves, wrong mud weight; fire if ignited
- ⚠ Hard to control — needs experts, days or weeks
### "Thirstwaves" — extreme evaporative demand
## 1. News
- Coined by: Meetpal S. Kukal (University of Idaho) and Mike Hobbins (University of Colorado/NOAA) — Earth's Future, March 2025
- Definition: three or more consecutive days of high atmospheric evaporative demand (EED)
- Measured by: standardised short-crop evapotranspiration (ET₀) — theoretical 12 cm grass crop
- Drivers: temperature, low humidity, wind, solar radiation — ⚠ not temperature alone; ⚠ not about rainfall
- India studies: Chattopadhyay & Hulme (1997) — PET declined 1960s–90s; IIT Roorkee & NIH (2022) — rising actual ET in North India, Himalayas
| Aspect | Heatwave | Thirstwave |
| Based on | high air temperature | high evaporative demand |
| Impact | human health | crop water stress |

## 1. News

## 1. News

| Montha | Thailand | preceding major post-monsoon cyclone; landfall on Kakinada coast, AP |
| Ditwah | Yemen |  |

- Senyar affected the habitat of the Tapanuli orangutan (Sumatra)
- AP got ₹341.48 crore additional NDRF aid for Montha (March 2026)

### Dugong
## Dugong
- India's only herbivorous marine mammal ("sea cow")
- IUCN Vulnerable; CITES Appendix I; Schedule I of Wildlife (Protection) Act; not found along the whole Indian coast
- Palk Bay Dugong Conservation Reserve (2022) — 448 sq km; India's first dedicated dugong conservation area
- World Dugong Day — 28 May
- Largest Indian population — Gulf of Mannar–Palk Bay region

## Other facts
- India's 1,100+ protected areas cover just over 5% of its area
- Northeast monsoon — the retreating monsoon; about three cyclonic disturbances per season in the Bay of Bengal
- Rain-fed areas — 60% of net sown area; NMSA, PKVY, PMFBY linked to climate-resilient agriculture
- Thirstwave — main impact crop water stress
### SAIEE 2025: first DNA elephant count
## SAIEE 2025
- India's first DNA-based elephant count — dung samples, mark-recapture model

- Largest population — Karnataka 6,013; Assam 4,159; Tamil Nadu 3,136; Kerala 2,785

- 2025 monsoon excess — Northwest India 27%, Central 15%, South Peninsula 10%
- NE monsoon gives 48% of Tamil Nadu's annual rainfall; over 30% of AP's
- Dudhia bridge (Siliguri–Mirik link) over the Balason washed away (Oct 2025)
- NITI Aayog — about 600 million Indians face high to extreme water stress
- India's urban population — nearly 1 billion by 2050
## Glaciers and GLOFs
- Glacial Lakes and Water Bodies Monitoring Report (June 2025) — Central Water Commission
- 432 of 681 glacial lakes expanding
- IPCC AR6 — about two-thirds of Himalayan glaciers may vanish by 2100 (high emissions)
## Climate
- COP28 (Dubai, 2023) — "transition away" from fossil fuels; 1.5°C budget may run out in two years
- India's 2030 NDC — emissions intensity cut 45% from 2005; non-fossil capacity 50%; carbon sink 2.5–3 billion tonnes
- Unmet climate-finance promise — $100 billion a year
- Surface solar radiation — Indo-Gangetic Plains and North-East may see a small rise under low emissions
- Offshore aquifers — about 10% of terrestrial freshwater, about 1 million km³

- Of 353 million tonnes plastic waste — 9% recycled, 19% incinerated; packaging 40%

### Hayli Gubbi volcano erupts after ~12,000 years
## 1. News
- Volcano: Hayli Gubbi, north-eastern Ethiopia (near Red Sea, Afar Rift)
- Erupted: 23 November 2025 — ⚠ first in nearly 12,000 years
- Type: explosive — ash and gases, no lava flows
- Ash plume: via Arabian Peninsula, Arabian Sea → Gujarat, Rajasthan → Delhi, UP → towards China; at 10–14 km altitude — flights disrupted
## 2. Background
| Type | Example |
| Active | Kilauea (Hawaii), Santa Maria (Guatemala) |
| Dormant | Mount Kilimanjaro (Tanzania) |
| Extinct | Dhinodhar Hill (Gujarat) |
| Shield (basaltic, gentle) | Mauna Loa |
| Composite / Stratovolcano (explosive) | Mayon, Mount Fuji, Mount Rainier |
- Mid-ocean ridge system: over 65,000 km
### Monsoon 2025 — 8% above normal, floods
## 1. Data
- Overall: 8% above normal (LPA 87 cm)
| Region | Excess |
| Northwest India | 27% (highest) |
| Central India | 15% |
| South Peninsula | 10% |

- Floods/landslides: Himachal Pradesh, J&K, Punjab

- ⚠ Cloudburst (IMD): ≥100 mm rainfall in one hour over a small area (~20–30 sq km) — not 50 mm

### E-waste and public health in India
## 1. Burden
- 2025: India generated 2.2 million MT — ⚠ third-largest after China and USA
- Growth: 150% rise from 0.71 million MT (2017–18); projected to double by 2030
- 65 cities produce >60%; hotspots Seelampur, Mustafabad (Delhi), Moradabad (UP), Bhiwandi (Maharashtra)
- Formal units: 322 registered recyclers (capacity 2.2 MT); only 43% processed formally (2023–24)
- PM2.5 in Seelampur > 300 μg/m³ — 12× WHO 24-hour limit (25 μg/m³)
## 2. Background
- E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022: Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR); mandatory registration of recyclers/dismantlers
- WHO: 18 million children and 13 million women in informal waste zones globally
- Released toxins: beryllium, cadmium, chromium, mercury, lead (UPSC 2013 PYQ)

- Reports: "Sustainable Food Systems: An Agenda for Climate-Risked Times" and "Missing from the Plate: Edible Plants in Wetlands Lose Favour"

### OCI report "Planet Wreckers" — Global North fossil expansion
## 1. Findings
- By: Oil Change International (OCI) — Australia, Canada, Norway, USA
- Oil & gas output up 14 million boe/day (2015–2024) — +40%
- ⚠ USA — over 90% of net global increase (+11 million boe/d); Australia +77% — highest growth rate among top 15; rest of world -2%
- Climate finance paid since 2015: only $280 billion vs need $1–5 trillion/year; fossil subsidies $465 billion
- Six majors (ExxonMobil, Chevron, Shell, TotalEnergies, BP, Eni) — profits $580 billion+
- COP28 (Dubai, 2023): "transition away" from fossil fuels; 1.5°C budget may be exhausted in two years

## 1. News

### Emissions may cut surface solar radiation over India by up to 10%
## 1. News
- Study: Ashwin Jadhav and Rohini Bhawar, Savitribai Phule Pune University — journal Energy and Climate Change
- Finding: up to 10% fall in surface solar radiation (SSR) over central, southern, eastern India under intermediate/high emissions — due to aerosols, turbidity, post-monsoon clouds
- Mean SSR loss: ~1.10 W/m² per decade (intermediate), ~1.32 W/m² per decade (high)
- ⚠ Indo-Gangetic Plains and NE may see marginal increase (low emission); north-west India most stable
- India solar capacity: 123,130.13 MW (31 Aug 2025); renewables 37% of capacity but ~18% of generation

- Beneath the Atlantic from New Jersey to Maine; not refilled by present rainfall
- Held in place by a clay cap rock; origins — Ice Age recharge, link to onshore aquifers

### India's urban future — climate-resilient cities
## 1. Facts

- Urban population: near 1 billion by 2050
- Housing: 144 million new homes needed by 2070; more than half of 2070 housing stock yet to be built
- Flood losses: $5 billion by 2030 → $30 billion by 2070; over 25% urban roads exposed to flooding
- Urban Heat Island: night temperatures 3–5°C higher
- Investment needed: ~$10.95 trillion over 30 years
- Examples: Kolkata flood forecasting; Chennai stormwater; Ahmedabad Heat Action Plan

### Oil and gas well blowouts — ONGC Sivasagar
## 1. News
- Event: blowout at an ONGC well in Sivasagar district, Assam on 12 June 2025
- Evacuated: over 1,500 residents
- Experts: well-control experts from the United States
- Controlled: by 21 June
## 2. Background
- Blowout: uncontrolled release of gas/oil when well pressure beats blowout preventers (BOPs) and drilling mud
- Causes: faulty valves, wrong mud weight; fire if ignited
- ⚠ Hard to control — needs experts, days or weeks
### Sea slug Phidiana unilineata rediscovered off Visakhapatnam after 160 years
## 1. News
- Species: Phidiana unilineata — a nudibranch (soft-bodied marine gastropod mollusc)
- When: City Nature Challenge 2025 (April) — global citizen science initiative
- Found by: Sri Chakra Pranav, founder, East Coast Conservation Team (ECCT)
- First recorded: 1864, from Visakhapatnam, by British zoologists Joshua Alder and Albany Hancock
- ⚠ No sighting since 1864 — possible endemic to Visakhapatnam coast (rocky shores, hydroids)
- Alder and Hancock recorded over 20 sea slug species from Visakhapatnam in the 19th century
- Indicator: sea slugs indicate healthy marine ecosystems (depend on corals, hydroids)

- 1st in Asia, 3rd in world
- 26 sites (2012) → 100; 56 added after 2020 (⚠ other source: 26 in 2014)

### India's 100th Ramsar Site — Surha Tal
## 1. What and where
- Site: Jayaprakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary (Surha Tal) — Ballia district, Uttar Pradesh
- Milestone: India's 100th Ramsar Site (June 2026)
- Type: freshwater wetland — habitat for migratory and resident birds
- Roles: ecological balance, groundwater recharge, fisheries, local livelihoods
## 2. Exam angle
- ⚠ 99th was Shekha Jheel Bird Sanctuary, Aligarh (UP) — 22 April 2026
- ⚠ 97th–98th: Patna Bird Sanctuary (Etah, UP) and Chhari-Dhand (Kutch, Gujarat) — World Wetlands Day 2026
- ⚠ With these, Uttar Pradesh has 13 sites — second after Tamil Nadu (20)

### India and the Ramsar Convention — key facts
## 1. Convention
- Signed: 1971 at Ramsar, Iran — wetlands of international importance
- India joined: 1 February 1982
## 2. India's numbers
- Total sites: 100 (June 2026); area about 14 lakh hectares
- Rank: 1st in Asia, 3rd in the world
- Growth: 26 sites (2012) → 98 (January 2026) → 100 (June 2026); 56 added after 2020
- ⚠ One source gives 26 sites in 2014 (growth over 276% to 98); the other says 2012
## 3. Records
| Record | Site |
| First sites (1981) | Chilika Lake (Odisha), Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan) |
| Largest | Sundarbans, West Bengal |
| Smallest | Vembannur Wetland Complex, Tamil Nadu (earlier: Renuka Wetland, Himachal Pradesh) |
| Most sites | Tamil Nadu – 20 |
| Second | Uttar Pradesh – 13 (after Surha Tal) |
| Only site in Andhra Pradesh | Kolleru Lake (2002) |
- ⚠ Older list (98 sites) shows UP – 11; it became 13 with Shekha Jheel and Surha Tal

# NOTES OF APP TOPIC 6:82 - Overview and data: Environment in news - reports, indices, species, protected areas, climate negotiations
### Indices → agency → India
- EPI 2026 puts India 176th of 177; CCPI 2026 puts it 23rd: two opposite pictures
| Index | Agency | India |
| EPI 2026 | Yale + Columbia | 176th of 177; Estonia 1st |
| CCPI 2026 | Germanwatch, NewClimate, CAN | 23rd (down 13) |
| SEEI 2024 | BEE | Andhra Pradesh top (87.25) |
| SEEI 2023 | BEE | Karnataka top |
| IQAir 2025 | Switzerland | India 6th; worst city Loni (UP) |
| Living Planet | WWF | 69% wildlife decline |
### UNFCCC COPs
| COP | Venue | Outcome |
| COP28 (2023) | Dubai | Global Stocktake; GRCA; L&D Fund |
| COP29 (2024) | Baku | NCQG US$300 bn/yr by 2035 |
| COP30 (2025) | Belém | First COP in Amazon; US$1.3 trn roadmap; TFFF |
| COP31 (2026) | Antalya | Australia presides |
| COP33 (2028) | - | India withdrew bid |
### Other convention COPs
| COP | Venue |
| CITES CoP20 | Samarkand; India blocked Guggul listing |
| Ramsar COP15 | Victoria Falls; Jayshree Vencatesan award |
| CMS COP15 | Campo Grande, Brazil |
| UNCCD COP17 | Ulaanbaatar, Mongolia |
### Alliances
- GRCA
- National Mission for Clean Ganga
- Launched COP28 Dubai, not New Delhi
- MAC
- UAE + Indonesia
- COP27; mangroves
- TFFF
- Brazil, US$125 billion
- India observer
- NDCTIA
- India, China, Vietnam
### Protected areas
- PA network: 109 NPs, 573 WLS, 58 TRs, 33 ERs, 18 BRs
- 58th TR: Madhav NP (Shivpuri, MP); MP most (9)
- Cold Desert BR (HP): India's 13th in UNESCO network
### Species & schemes
- Sphaerotheca varshaabhu: burrowing frog, Karnataka (PYQ)
- Impatiens karuppusamyi: plant, BSI (PYQ)
- Gaja Mitra: Karnataka (PYQ); first AI elephant-conflict centre: Palamu TR
- Operation Olivia: Coast Guard, Olive Ridley
- Green Credit: non-tradable; MISHTI: AP 2nd
### Exam corner
- Germanwatch = CCPI + CRI; Yale + Columbia = EPI
- Most polluted city: 2024 Byrnihat; 2025 Loni
- US$300 bn = COP29; US$1.3 trn = COP30
- COP15: Ramsar = Victoria Falls; CMS = Campo Grande
- Trinidad and Tobago: emergency over oil spill (PYQ)
- Carbon-negative: Bhutan, Suriname, Panama
### New species in news — other species → type, location
## 1. Species → type → location
| Species (news date) | Type | Location / note |
| Impatiens karuppusamyi (PYQ, asked 2024) | New plant species | Described by the Botanical Survey of India (BSI) |
| Sphaerotheca varshaabhu (PYQ) | New burrowing frog | Urban landscape of Karnataka (Bengaluru) |
| Rakthamichthys anchi (Aug 2026) | Fauna, blind, blood-red swamp eel | Dug-out wells, Goalpara, Assam (LENSD, 6 Aug 2026) |
| Dipturus dhritiae (Sep 2026) | Deep-sea skate (Deep Ocean Mission) | Arabian Sea; named after Dhriti Banerjee, ZSI's first woman Director |
| Lycodon irwini (2025) | Irwin's wolf snake | Great Nicobar Island |
| Five Allographa species (Jul 2026) | Lichen-forming fungi | Western Ghats |
| Piratula acuminata | Wolf spider | Sundarbans (APPCA 2026) |
| Three Conocephalus katydids | Insects | Alpine meadows, J&K |
- Exam angle: "new species - correct pair" questions match name with taxon (plant/frog/spider) and the discovering body (BSI/ZSI).
- ⚠ Hamataliwa papikonda = spider in AP (Papikonda NP); Ammannia telanganensis = plant in Telangana
- ⚠ Simonia lawbah = India's first Simonia (spider); Mesalina bishnoi = first Mesalina (lizard)
### Species conservation — schemes, centres and status
## 1. Schemes and centres → place
| Measure | Place / fact |
| 'Gaja Mitra' scheme | For human-elephant conflict; approved by the Karnataka Cabinet (PYQ; asked 2025) |
| India's first AI-based human-elephant conflict research centre | Palamu TR, Jharkhand |
| AP tiger relocation | 4 female tigers to be brought from Maharashtra (Chandrapur) + MP |
| Operation Olivia | Indian Coast Guard protection of Olive Ridley mass nesting + Turtle Excluder Devices in nets |
## 2. Big mammals and turtles
- Kaziranga (rhino): SC's Central Empowered Committee pulled up Assam over mining near the park (Aug 2026)
- Olive Ridley: Arribada at Gahirmatha and Rushikulya (Odisha); threat = coastal erosion of nesting beaches (Aug 2026)
## 3. IUCN Red List changes
| Species | Change | When |
| Numbat | Downlisted: EN → NT | IUCN Red List 2026-1 (Jul 2026) |
| Desert rain frog | Uplisted: NT → VU | IUCN Red List 2026-1 (Jul 2026) |
| Emperor penguin + Antarctic fur seal | Uplisted to Endangered | Earlier (Apr 2026) |
### Protected areas — designations, bodies and the PA network
## 1. Designations → body → latest status
| Designation | Body | Latest status (date) |
| Tiger reserve | NTCA | 58 reserves; Madhav NP (Shivpuri, MP) = 58th; MP has the most (9) |
| UNESCO biosphere reserve | UNESCO MAB | Cold Desert BR (Lahaul-Spiti, HP) = India's 13th in the World Network, Sep 2025 (37th MAB ICC, Hangzhou); India has 18 BRs in all |
| Hope Spot | Mission Blue (with IUCN) | Not in your sources: any Hope Spot declared in 2025-26 |
- ⚠ 13th = UNESCO World Network BRs; 18 = all BRs in India
## 2. Data — the PA network
- PA network (2026): 109 NPs, 573 WLS, 58 TRs, 33 Elephant Reserves, 18 Biosphere Reserves
### Alliances and initiatives → lead country, members, objective
| Initiative | Sponsor / members | Objective |
| Global River Cities Alliance (GRCA) | Led by the National Mission for Clean Ganga (Ministry of Jal Shakti); launched at COP28, Dubai (Dec 2023), not New Delhi | - |
| Mangrove Alliance for Climate (MAC) | Led by UAE and Indonesia, launched at COP27 (Sharm el-Sheikh, 2022); India is a member [GK] | Conserve and restore mangroves |
| International Nitrogen Initiative | Set up in 2003 under SCOPE and the International Geosphere-Biosphere Programme (IGBP) [GK] | Optimise nitrogen use, cut nitrogen pollution |
| NDCTIA (NDC Transport Initiative for Asia) | Funded by Germany's International Climate Initiative (IKI); India, China and Vietnam [GK] | Decarbonise transport in line with NDCs |
| Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF) | Brazil's US$125 billion forest fund launched at COP30; India joined as an observer (2026) | Pay for standing tropical forests |
| "35 by 35" | COP31 (Australia-Turkey co-presidency, Antalya) | Electricity to be 35% of final energy by 2035 |
- ⚠ IBCA HQ = New Delhi; GRCA launched at COP28 Dubai, not New Delhi
- ⚠ MAC = UAE + Indonesia at COP27; TFFF = Brazil at COP30
